#+title: Org's options in Emacs
#+options: ^:{} toc:1

What only Emacs can do, the server has Emacs do: export, evaluating code
blocks, attachments, citations, encryption, LaTeX previews and a few
others ([[file:configuration.org::#export-babel-and-other-work-in-emacs][Export, Babel and other work in Emacs]]). The options of Org that
Emacs reads for these are set in ~emacs_options~, by their names in Org.
This page lists each of them, with Org's own documentation of it.

The other options of Org are not here: most are settings of the server
([[file:configuration.org][configuration.org]]), and those whose value is Lisp (a function, a hook)
go in ~emacs_config~. [[file:parity.org::#options][The Options section of parity.org]] says what
org-lsp does with each.

* Setting them

In the shared file, under ~[emacs_options]~:

#+begin_src toml
# ~/.config/org-lsp/config.toml
[emacs_options]
org-attach-method = "mv"
org-export-with-toc = false
org-export-backends = ["ascii", "html", "icalendar", "latex", "md", "odt"]
org-babel-load-languages = { emacs-lisp = true, python = true }
#+end_src

From an editor, as the other options: in Neovim, whose names with ~-~
need brackets,

#+begin_src lua
require('org-lsp').setup({
  settings = {
    emacs_options = { ['org-attach-method'] = 'mv', ['org-export-with-toc'] = false },
  },
})
#+end_src

and in VS Code's ~settings.json~, which completes the names and shows
the first sentence of each one's documentation:

#+begin_src json
"org-lsp.settings": {
  "emacs_options": { "org-attach-method": "mv", "org-export-with-toc": false }
}
#+end_src

The editor's ~emacs_options~ goes over the file's option by option.

* How values become Lisp

The values are JSON (or TOML), which Emacs makes what the option's type
(its ~custom-type~) wants:

- ~true~ is ~t~ and ~false~ is ~nil~;
- a number is a number, and an integer where the type wants a float is
  that float;
- a string is a string, or a symbol where the type wants one (~"mv"~ is
  ~mv~), or a character where it wants one;
- an array is a list;
- a table is an association list, its keys made what the type wants
  (~{ emacs-lisp = true }~ is ~((emacs-lisp . t))~), or a list of its
  pairs where the type wants a list, or a property list, whose keys are
  keywords (~scale~ is ~:scale~), where it wants one.

Emacs then checks the value against the type, as Customize does. A value
the option does not take is left out, with a message that says what it
takes; the server names an option that is not one of those here, and
says why it leaves it out.

* When they apply

Emacs loads ~emacs_config_file~, evaluates ~emacs_config~, then loads Org.
Each option of ~emacs_options~ is set when the library it is in loads
(~org-attach-method~ when ~org-attach~ does), as Customize sets it, with
the option's own ~:set~ function: so after ~emacs_config_file~ and
~emacs_config~, and over what they set.

The server starts Emacs with them the first time it needs it and keeps it
running: after changing them, restart the server ([[file:configuration.org::#when-something-is-wrong][When something is wrong]]
says how in each editor).

Lisp, which ~emacs_options~ cannot say, goes in ~emacs_config~: a
function (~org-html-format-drawer-function~), a hook, or any value
computed when Emacs runs.

#+begin_src toml
emacs_config = '''
(setq org-html-format-drawer-function #'my-drawer)
'''
#+end_src

* The options
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: the-options
:END:

# BEGIN generated: options

The 480 options, by the file of Org they are in. Each has Org's own
documentation of it, as ~C-h v~ shows it in Emacs, then its default as
Emacs prints it, its type as Customize has it, and its default in the
shared file, where TOML can say it.

** ob-C.el

*** org-babel-C-compiler
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-babel-c-compiler
:END:

Command used to compile a C source code file into an executable.
May be either a command in the path, like gcc
or an absolute path name, like ~/usr/local/bin/gcc~
parameter may be used, like gcc -v

- Default :: ~"gcc"~
- Type :: ~string~
- Since :: Emacs 24.3

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-babel-C-compiler = "gcc"
#+end_src

*** org-babel-C++-compiler
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-babel-c-compiler-1
:END:

Command used to compile a C++ source code file into an executable.
May be either a command in the path, like g++
or an absolute path name, like ~/usr/local/bin/g++~
parameter may be used, like g++ -v

- Default :: ~"g++"~
- Type :: ~string~
- Since :: Emacs 24.3

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
"org-babel-C++-compiler" = "g++"
#+end_src

*** org-babel-D-compiler
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-babel-d-compiler
:END:

Command used to compile and execute a D source code file.
May be either a command in the path, like rdmd
or an absolute path name, like ~/usr/local/bin/rdmd~
parameter may be used, like rdmd --chatty

- Default :: ~"rdmd"~
- Type :: ~string~
- Since :: Emacs 24.3

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-babel-D-compiler = "rdmd"
#+end_src

** ob-clojure.el

*** org-babel-clojure-backend
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-babel-clojure-backend
:END:

Backend used to evaluate Clojure code blocks.

- Default :: computed when Org loads:
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (cond
   ((executable-find "bb") 'babashka)
   ((executable-find "clojure") 'clojure-cli)
   ((featurep 'cider) 'cider)
   ((featurep 'inf-clojure) 'inf-clojure)
   ((featurep 'slime) 'slime)
   (t nil))
  #+end_src
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (choice
   (const :tag "babashka" babashka)
   (const :tag "clojure-cli" clojure-cli)
   (const :tag "cider" cider)
   (const :tag "inf-clojure" inf-clojure)
   (const :tag "slime" slime)
   (const :tag "Not configured yet" nil))
  #+end_src
- Since :: Org 9.7

*** org-babel-clojurescript-backend
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-babel-clojurescript-backend
:END:

Backend used to evaluate ClojureScript code blocks.

- Default :: computed when Org loads:
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (cond
   ((or (executable-find "nbb") (executable-find "npx")) 'nbb)
   ((featurep 'cider) 'cider)
   (t nil))
  #+end_src
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (choice
   (const :tag "nbb" nbb)
   (const :tag "cider" cider)
   (const :tag "Not configured yet" nil))
  #+end_src
- Since :: Org 9.7

*** org-babel-clojure-default-ns
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-babel-clojure-default-ns
:END:

Default Clojure namespace for source block when finding ns failed.

- Default :: ~"user"~
- Type :: ~string~

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-babel-clojure-default-ns = "user"
#+end_src

*** ob-clojure-babashka-command
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: ob-clojure-babashka-command
:END:

Babashka command used by the Clojure ~babashka~ backend.

- Default :: computed when Org loads: ~(executable-find "bb")~
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (choice file
          (const nil))
  #+end_src
- Since :: Org 9.6

*** ob-clojure-nbb-command
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: ob-clojure-nbb-command
:END:

Nbb command used by the ClojureScript ~nbb~ backend.

- Default :: computed when Org loads:
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (or (executable-find "nbb")
      (when-let* ((npx (executable-find "npx")))
        (concat npx " nbb")))
  #+end_src
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (choice string
          (const nil))
  #+end_src
- Since :: Org 9.7

*** ob-clojure-cli-command
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: ob-clojure-cli-command
:END:

Clojure CLI command used by the Clojure ~clojure-cli~ backend.

- Default :: computed when Org loads:
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (when-let* ((cmd (executable-find "clojure")))
    (concat cmd " -M"))
  #+end_src
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (choice string
          (const nil))
  #+end_src
- Since :: Org 9.7

** ob-core.el

*** org-confirm-babel-evaluate
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-confirm-babel-evaluate
:END:

Confirm before evaluation.

Uses keymap ~org-mode-map~, which is not currently defined.
Require confirmation before interactively evaluating code
blocks in Org buffers.  The default value of this variable is t,
meaning confirmation is required for any code block evaluation.
This variable can be set to nil to inhibit any future
confirmation requests.  This variable can also be set to a
function which takes two arguments the language of the code block
and the body of the code block.  Such a function should then
return a non-nil value if the user should be prompted for
execution or nil if no prompt is required.

Warning: Disabling confirmation may result in accidental
evaluation of potentially harmful code.  It may be advisable
remove code block execution from ~M-x org-ctrl-c-ctrl-c~ as further protection
against accidental code block evaluation.  The
~org-babel-no-eval-on-ctrl-c-ctrl-c~ variable can be used to
remove code block execution from the ~M-x org-ctrl-c-ctrl-c~ keybinding.

- Default :: ~t~
- Type :: ~(choice boolean function)~
- Since :: Emacs 24.1

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-confirm-babel-evaluate = true
#+end_src

*** org-babel-no-eval-on-ctrl-c-ctrl-c
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-babel-no-eval-on-ctrl-c-ctrl-c
:END:

Uses keymap ~org-mode-map~, which is not currently defined.
Remove code block evaluation from the ~M-x org-ctrl-c-ctrl-c~ key binding.

- Default :: ~nil~
- Type :: ~boolean~
- Since :: Emacs 24.1

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-babel-no-eval-on-ctrl-c-ctrl-c = false
#+end_src

*** org-babel-results-keyword
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-babel-results-keyword
:END:

Keyword used to name results generated by code blocks.
It should be "RESULTS".  However any capitalization may be
used.

- Default :: ~"RESULTS"~
- Type :: ~string~
- Since :: Org 8.0

#+begin_src toml

org-babel-results-keyword = "RESULTS"
#+end_src

*** org-babel-inline-result-wrap
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-babel-inline-result-wrap
:END:

Format string used to wrap inline results.
This string must include a "%s" which will be replaced by the results.

- Default :: ~"=%s="~
- Type :: ~string~

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-babel-inline-result-wrap = "=%s="
#+end_src

:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-babel-hash-show-time
:END:

Non-nil means show the time the code block was evaluated in the result hash.

- Default :: ~nil~
- Type :: ~boolean~

#+begin_src toml

#+end_src

:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-babel-uppercase-example-markers
:END:

When non-nil, begin/end example markers will be inserted in upper case.

- Default :: ~nil~

#+begin_src toml

** ob-csharp.el

*** org-babel-csharp-compiler
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-babel-csharp-compiler
:END:

The program to call for compiling a csharp project.

- Default :: ~"dotnet"~
- Type :: ~string~
- Since :: Org. (9.8)

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]

#+end_src

*** org-babel-csharp-default-target-framework
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-babel-csharp-default-target-framework
:END:

The desired target framework to use.

- Default :: computed when Org loads:
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (format "net%s.0"
          (let ((net-sdks (org-babel-csharp--find-dotnet-version)))
            (when net-sdks
              (apply #'max net-sdks))))
  #+end_src
- Type :: ~string~
- Since :: Org. (9.8)

*** org-babel-csharp-generate-compile-command
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-babel-csharp-generate-compile-command
:END:

A function creating the compile command.

It must take two parameters intended for the target binary directory and
a .sln file, .csproj file, or a base directory where either can be found.

- Default :: ~org-babel-csharp--default-compile-command~
- Type :: ~function~
- Since :: Org. (9.8)

*** org-babel-csharp-generate-restore-command
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-babel-csharp-generate-restore-command
:END:

A function creating a project restore command.

It must take one parameter defining the project to perform a restore on.

- Default :: ~org-babel-csharp--default-restore-command~
- Type :: ~function~
- Since :: Org. (9.8)

*** org-babel-csharp-additional-project-flags
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-babel-csharp-additional-project-flags
:END:

Will be passed in the "PropertyGroup" defining the project.

This is taken as-is. It should be a string in XML-format.

- Default :: ~nil~
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (choice string
          (const nil))
  #+end_src
- Since :: Org. (9.8)

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-babel-csharp-additional-project-flags = false
#+end_src

** ob-ditaa.el

*** org-ditaa-default-exec-mode
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-ditaa-default-exec-mode
:END:

Method to use for ditaa diagram generation when generating png or svg output.
~jar~ means to use java together with a JAR.
The JAR must be set via ~org-ditaa-jar-path~.

~ditaa~ means to use the ditaa executable.
The executable can be configured via ~org-ditaa-exec~.

- Default :: ~jar~
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (choice
   (const :tag "Use java together with a JAR file." jar)
   (const :tag "Use ditaa executable." ditaa))
  #+end_src
- Since :: Org 9.8

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-ditaa-default-exec-mode = "jar"
#+end_src

*** org-ditaa-exec
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-ditaa-exec
:END:

File name of the ditaa executable.

- Default :: ~"ditaa"~
- Type :: ~string~
- Since :: Org 9.8

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-ditaa-exec = "ditaa"
#+end_src

*** org-ditaa-java-exec
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-ditaa-java-exec
:END:

Java executable to use when evaluating ditaa blocks using a JAR.

- Default :: ~"java"~
- Type :: ~string~
- Since :: Org 9.8

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-ditaa-java-exec = "java"
#+end_src

*** org-ditaa-jar-path
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-ditaa-jar-path
:END:

Path to the ditaa.jar file.

- Default :: computed when Org loads:
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (expand-file-name
   "ditaa.jar"
   (file-name-as-directory
    (expand-file-name
     "scripts"
     (file-name-as-directory
      (expand-file-name
       "../contrib"
       (file-name-directory (org-find-library-dir "org")))))))
  #+end_src
- Type :: ~string~

*** org-ditaa-eps-jar-path
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-ditaa-eps-jar-path
:END:

Path to the DitaaEps.jar executable.
Used when generating eps or pdf output.

- Default :: computed when Org loads:
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (expand-file-name "DitaaEps.jar" (file-name-directory org-ditaa-jar-path))
  #+end_src
- Type :: ~string~
- Since :: Org 8.0

** ob-exp.el

*** org-export-use-babel
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-use-babel
:END:

Switch controlling code evaluation and header processing during export.
When set to nil no code will be evaluated as part of the export
process and no header arguments will be obeyed.  Users who wish
to avoid evaluating code on export should use the header argument
~:eval never-export~.

- Default :: ~t~
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (choice
   (const :tag "Never" nil)
   (const :tag "Always" t))
  #+end_src
- Since :: Emacs 24.1

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-export-use-babel = true
#+end_src

*** org-babel-exp-call-line-template
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-babel-exp-call-line-template
:END:

Template used to export call lines.
This template may be customized to include the call line name
with any export markup.  The template is filled out using
~org-fill-template~, and the following %keys may be used.

#+begin_example
line --- call line
#+end_example

An example value would be "~\n~: call: %line" to export the call line
wrapped in a verbatim environment.

Note: the results are inserted separately after the contents of
this template.

- Default :: ~""~
- Type :: ~string~

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-babel-exp-call-line-template = ""
#+end_src

*** org-babel-exp-code-template
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-babel-exp-code-template
:END:

Template used to export the body of code blocks.
This template may be customized to include additional information
such as the code block name, or the values of particular header
arguments.  The template is filled out using ~org-fill-template~,
and the following %keys may be used.

#+begin_example
lang ------ the language of the code block
name ------ the name of the code block
body ------ the body of the code block
switches -- the switches associated to the code block
header-args the header arguments of the code block
#+end_example

In addition to the keys mentioned above, every header argument
defined for the code block may be used as a key and will be
replaced with its value.

- Default :: ~"#+begin_src %lang%switches%header-args\n%body\n#+end_src"~
- Type :: ~string~
- Since :: Org 9.7

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-babel-exp-code-template = "#+begin_src %lang%switches%header-args\n%body\n#+end_src"
#+end_src

*** org-babel-exp-inline-code-template
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-babel-exp-inline-code-template
:END:

Template used to export the body of inline code blocks.
This template may be customized to include additional information
such as the code block name, or the values of particular header
arguments.  The template is filled out using ~org-fill-template~,
and the following %keys may be used.

#+begin_example
lang ------ the language of the code block
name ------ the name of the code block
body ------ the body of the code block
switches -- the switches associated to the code block
header-args the header arguments of the code block
#+end_example

In addition to the keys mentioned above, every header argument
defined for the code block may be used as a key and will be
replaced with its value.

- Default :: ~"src_%lang[%switches%header-args]{%body}"~
- Type :: ~string~
- Since :: Org 9.7

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-babel-exp-inline-code-template = "src_%lang[%switches%header-args]{%body}"
#+end_src

*** org-babel-latex-process-alist
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-babel-latex-process-alist
:END:

Definitions of external processes for LaTeX result generation.
See ~org-preview-latex-process-alist~ for more details.

The following process symbols are recognized:
- ~png~ :: Process used to produce .png output.

- Default :: computed when Org loads:
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  `((png :programs ("latex" "dvipng") :description "dvi > png"
         :message
         "you need to install the programs: latex and dvipng."
         :image-input-type "dvi" :image-output-type "png"
         :image-size-adjust (1.0 . 1.0) :latex-compiler
         ,(if (and (executable-find "latexmk") (executable-find "perl"))
              '("latexmk -f -pdf -latex -interaction=nonstopmode -output-directory=%o %f")
            '("latex -interaction nonstopmode -output-directory %o %f"
              "latex -interaction nonstopmode -output-directory %o %f"
              "latex -interaction nonstopmode -output-directory %o %f"))
         :image-converter ("dvipng -D %D -T tight -o %O %f")
         :transparent-image-converter
         ("dvipng -D %D -T tight -bg Transparent -o %O %f")))
  #+end_src
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (alist :tag "LaTeX to image backends" :value-type
         (plist))
  #+end_src
- Since :: Org 9.8

** ob-lilypond.el

*** org-babel-lilypond-commands
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-babel-lilypond-commands
:END:

Commands to run lilypond and view or play the results.
These should be executables that take a filename as an argument.
On some system it is possible to specify the filename directly
and the viewer or player will be determined from the file type;
you can leave the string empty on this case.

- Default :: computed when Org loads:
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (cond
   ((eq system-type 'darwin)
    '("/Applications/lilypond.app/Contents/Resources/bin/lilypond" "open" "open"))
   ((eq system-type 'windows-nt)
    '("lilypond" "" ""))
   (t
    '("lilypond" "xdg-open" "xdg-open")))
  #+end_src
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (list
   (string :tag "Lilypond   ")
   (string :tag "PDF Viewer ")
   (string :tag "MIDI Player"))
  #+end_src
- Since :: Org 8.2.7

*** org-babel-shell-results-defaults-to-output
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-babel-shell-results-defaults-to-output
:END:

Let shell execution defaults to ":results output".

When set to t, use ":results output" when no :results setting
is set.  This is especially useful for inline source blocks.

When set to nil, stick to the convention of using :results value
as the default setting when no :results is set, the "value" of
a shell execution being its exit code.

- Default :: ~t~
- Type :: ~boolean~
- Since :: Org 9.4

#+begin_src toml

#+end_src

** oc-biblatex.el

*** org-cite-biblatex-options
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-cite-biblatex-options
:END:

Options added to "biblatex" package.
If "biblatex" package is already required in the document, e.g., through
~org-latex-packages-alist~ variable, these options are ignored.

- Default :: ~nil~
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (choice
   (string :tag "Options (key=value,key2=value2...)")
   (const :tag "No option" nil))
  #+end_src
- Since :: Org 9.5

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-cite-biblatex-options = false
#+end_src

*** org-cite-biblatex-styles
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-cite-biblatex-styles
:END:

List of styles and variants, with associated BibLaTeX commands.

Each style follows the pattern

#+begin_example
(NAME VARIANT COMMAND MULTI-COMMAND NO-OPTION)
#+end_example

where:

#+begin_example
NAME is the name of the style, as a string, or nil.  The nil
style is the default style.  As such, it must have an entry in
the list.

VARIANT is the name of the style variant, as a string or nil.
The nil variant is the default variant for the current style.
As such, each style name must be associated to a nil variant.

COMMAND is the LaTeX command to use, as a string.  It should
not contain the leading backslash character.

MULTI-COMMAND is the LaTeX command to use when a multi-cite
command is appropriate.  When nil, the style is deemed
inappropriate for multi-cites.  The command should not contain
the leading backslash character.

NO-OPTION is a boolean.  When non-nil, no optional argument
should be added to the LaTeX command.
#+end_example

Each NAME-VARIANT pair should be unique in the list.

It is also possible to provide shortcuts for style and variant
names.  See ~org-cite-biblatex-style-shortcuts~.

- Default ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (("author" "caps" "Citeauthor*" nil nil)
   ("author" "full" "citeauthor" nil nil)
   ("author" "caps-full" "Citeauthor" nil nil)
   ("author" nil "citeauthor*" nil nil)
   ("locators" "bare" "notecite" nil nil)
   ("locators" "caps" "Pnotecite" nil nil)
   ("locators" "bare-caps" "Notecite" nil nil)
   ("locators" nil "pnotecite" nil nil)
   ("noauthor" "bare" "cite*" nil nil)
   ("noauthor" nil "autocite*" nil nil)
   ("nocite" nil "nocite" nil t)
   ("text" "caps" "Textcite" "Textcites" nil)
   ("text" nil "textcite" "textcites" nil)
   (nil "bare" "cite" "cites" nil)
   (nil "caps" "Autocite" "Autocites" nil)
   (nil "bare-caps" "Cite" "Cites" nil)
   (nil nil "autocite" "autocites" nil))
  #+end_src
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (repeat
   (list :tag "Style/variant combination"
         (choice :tag "Style"
                 (string :tag "Name")
                 (const :tag "Default style" nil))
         (choice :tag "Variant"
                 (string :tag "Name")
                 (const :tag "Default variant" nil))
         (string :tag "Command name")
         (choice :tag "Multicite command"
                 (string :tag "Command name")
  …
  #+end_src
- Since :: Org 9.6

*** org-cite-biblatex-style-shortcuts
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-cite-biblatex-style-shortcuts
:END:

List of shortcuts associated to style or variant names.

Each entry is a pair (NAME . STYLE-NAME) where NAME is the name
of the shortcut, as a string, and STYLE-NAME is the name of
a style in ~org-cite-biblatex-styles~.

- Default ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (("a" . "author")
   ("b" . "bare")
   ("bc" . "bare-caps")
   ("c" . "caps")
   ("cf" . "caps-full")
   ("f" . "full")
   ("l" . "locators")
   ("n" . "nocite")
   ("na" . "noauthor")
   ("t" . "text"))
  #+end_src
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (repeat
   (cons :tag "Shortcut"
         (string :tag "Name")
         (string :tag "Full name")))
  #+end_src
- Since :: Org 9.6

** oc-bibtex.el

*** org-cite-bibtex-bibliography-style
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-cite-bibtex-bibliography-style
:END:

Default BibTeX bibliography style.

BibTeX provides the following default styles:

#+begin_example
"plain"   Sorted numbered entries
"abbrv"   Sorted numbered entries with abbreviated author name
"unsrt"   Unsorted numbered entries
"alpha"   Alphabetized entries with unique short id
"acm"     ACM Transactions
"apalike" APA-like
"ieeetr"  IEEE Transactions
"siam"    SIAM
#+end_example

- Default :: ~"plain"~
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (choice
   (const :tag "Plain" "plain")
   (const :tag "Abbreviated" "abbrv")
   (const :tag "Unsorted" "unsrt")
   (const :tag "Alphabetic" "alpha")
   (const :tag "ACM" "acm")
   (const :tag "APA-like" "apalike")
   (const :tag "IEEE" "ieeetr")
   (const :tag "SIAM" "siam")
   (string :tag "Other"))
  #+end_src
- Since :: Org 9.8

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-cite-bibtex-bibliography-style = "plain"
#+end_src

*** org-cite-csl-latex-label-separator
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-cite-csl-latex-label-separator
:END:

Distance between citation label and bibliography item for LaTeX output.
The value is a string representing the distance in valid LaTeX units.
Used only when ~second-field-align~ is activated by the used CSL
style.

The indentation length in these cases is computed as the sum of
~org-cite-csl-latex-label-separator~ and the maximal label width, for
example,

#+begin_example
    indentation length
<------------------------->
#+end_example

max.  label width  separator

#+begin_example
<---------------><-------->
[Doe22]                    John Doe.  A title...
[DoeSmithJones19]          John Doe, Jane Smith and...
[SmithDoe02]               Jane Smith and John Doe...
#+end_example

The maximal label width, in turn, is calculated as the product of
~org-cite-csl-latex-label-width-per-char~ and the maximal label
length measured in characters.

- Default :: ~"0.6em"~
- Type :: ~string~
- Since :: Org 9.7

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-cite-csl-latex-label-separator = "0.6em"
#+end_src

style.

See the documentation of ~org-cite-csl-latex-label-separator~ for
details.

- Type :: ~string~

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-cite-csl-latex-label-width-per-char = "0.45em"
#+end_src

*** org-cite-csl-latex-preamble
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-cite-csl-latex-preamble
:END:

LaTeX preamble content inserted by the ~csl~ citation processor.

This preamble can be anything as long as it provides definitions
for the environment and commands that Citeproc's ~org-latex~
formatter uses for formatting citations and bibliographies.  In
particular, it has to define
- the commands ~\cslblock{<text>}~, ~\cslleftmargin{<text>}~,
  ~\cslrightinline{<text>}~ and ~\cslindent{<text>}~ for formatting
  text that have, respectively, the CSL display attributes
  ~block~, ~left-margin~, ~right-inline~ and ~indent~;
- the commands ~\cslcitation{<item_no>}{<item_text>}~ and
  ~\cslbibitem{<item_no>}{<item_text>}~, which are used to
  format individual citations and bibliography items, including
  hyperlinking citations to the corresponding bibliography entry
  using their numerical id, which is passed as the first,
  <item_no> argument;
- and the environment ~\cslbibliography{<hanging-indent>}{<entry-spacing>}~,
  in which bibliographies are wrapped; the value of the
  <hanging-indent> argument is 1 if hanging indent should be
  applied and 0 if not, while the <entry-spacing> argument is an
  integer specifying the number of extra line-heights
  required between bibliography entries in addition to normal
  line spacing.

When present, the placeholders [CSL-HANGINDENT], [CSL-LABELSEP],
[CSL-LABELWIDTH-PER-CHAR] and [CSL-MAXLABEL-CHARS] are replaced,
respectively, by the contents of the customizable variables
~org-cite-csl-latex-hanging-indent~, ~org-cite-csl-latex-label-separator~,
~org-cite-csl-latex-label-width-per-char~, and the maximal label length
in the bibliography measured in characters.

- Default ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  "\\usepackage{calc}\n\\newlength{\\cslhangindent}\n\\setlength{\\cslhangindent}{[CSL-HANGINDENT]}\n\\newlength{\\csllabelsep}\n\\setlength{\\csllabelsep}{[CSL-LABELSEP]}\n\\newlength{\\csllabelwidth}\n\\setlength{\\csllabelwidth}{[CSL-LABELWIDTH-PER-CHAR] * [CSL-MAXLABEL-CHARS]}\n\\newenvironment{cslbibliography}[2] % 1st arg. is hanging-indent, 2nd entry spacing.\n {% By default, paragraphs are not indented.\n  \\setlength{\\parindent}{0pt}\n  % Hanging indent is turned on when first argument is 1.\n  \\ifodd #1\n  \\let\\oldpar\\par\n  \\def\\par{\\hangindent=\\cslhangindent\\oldpar}\n  \\fi\n  % Set entry spacing based on the second argument.\n  \\setlength{\\parskip}{\\parskip +  #2\\baselineskip}\n }%\n {}\n\\newcommand{\\cslblock}[1]{#1\\hfill\\break}\n\\newcommand{\\cslleftmargin}[1]{\\parbox[t]{\\csllabelsep + \\csllabelwidth}{#1}}\n\\newcommand{\\cslrightinline}[1]\n  {\\parbox[t]{\\linewidth - \\csllabelsep - \\csllabelwidth}{#1}\\break}\n\\newcommand{\\cslindent}[1]{\\hspace{\\cslhangindent}#1}\n\\newcommand{\\cslbibitem}[2]\n  {\\leavevmode\\vadjust pre{\\hypertarget{citeproc_bib_item_#1}{}}#2}\n\\makeatletter\n\\newcommand{\\cslcitation}[2]\n {\\protect\\hyper@linkstart{cite}{citeproc_bib_item_#1}#2\\hyper@linkend}\n\\makeatother"
  #+end_src
- Type :: ~string~
- Since :: Org 9.7

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-cite-csl-latex-preamble = "\\usepackage{calc}\n\\newlength{\\cslhangindent}\n\\setlength{\\cslhangindent}{[CSL-HANGINDENT]}\n\\newlength{\\csllabelsep}\n\\setlength{\\csllabelsep}{[CSL-LABELSEP]}\n\\newlength{\\csllabelwidth}\n\\setlength{\\csllabelwidth}{[CSL-LABELWIDTH-PER-CHAR] * [CSL-MAXLABEL-CHARS]}\n\\newenvironment{cslbibliography}[2] % 1st arg. is hanging-indent, 2nd entry spacing.\n {% By default, paragraphs are not indented.\n  \\setlength{\\parindent}{0pt}\n  % Hanging indent is turned on when first argument is 1.\n  \\ifodd #1\n  \\let\\oldpar\\par\n  \\def\\par{\\hangindent=\\cslhangindent\\oldpar}\n  \\fi\n  % Set entry spacing based on the second argument.\n  \\setlength{\\parskip}{\\parskip +  #2\\baselineskip}\n }%\n {}\n\\newcommand{\\cslblock}[1]{#1\\hfill\\break}\n\\newcommand{\\cslleftmargin}[1]{\\parbox[t]{\\csllabelsep + \\csllabelwidth}{#1}}\n\\newcommand{\\cslrightinline}[1]\n  {\\parbox[t]{\\linewidth - \\csllabelsep - \\csllabelwidth}{#1}\\break}\n\\newcommand{\\cslindent}[1]{\\hspace{\\cslhangindent}#1}\n\\newcommand{\\cslbibitem}[2]\n  {\\leavevmode\\vadjust pre{\\hypertarget{citeproc_bib_item_#1}{}}#2}\n\\makeatletter\n\\newcommand{\\cslcitation}[2]\n {\\protect\\hyper@linkstart{cite}{citeproc_bib_item_#1}#2\\hyper@linkend}\n\\makeatother"
#+end_src

*** org-cite-csl-bibtex-titles-to-sentence-case
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-cite-csl-bibtex-titles-to-sentence-case
:END:

Convert bibtex title fields to sentence-case by default.

When non-nil, title fields in bibtex bibliography entries are
converted to sentence-case before being formatted according to a
CSL style, except for entries with a ~langid~ field specifying a
non-English language.  When nil, title conversion is limited to
entries having a ~langid~ field specifying a variant of English.

Conversion of titles to sentence-case by default is in most cases
useful because the CSL standard assumes that English titles are
specified in sentence-case but the bibtex bibliography format
requires them to be written in title-case.

- Default :: ~t~
- Type :: ~boolean~
- Since :: Org 9.8

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-cite-csl-bibtex-titles-to-sentence-case = true
#+end_src

** oc.el

*** org-cite-export-processors
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-cite-export-processors
:END:

Processor used for exporting citations, as a triplet, or nil.

When nil, citations and bibliography are not exported.

When non-nil, the value is an association list between export backends and
citation export processors:

#+begin_example
(BACKEND . PROCESSOR)
#+end_example

where BACKEND is the name of an export backend or t, and PROCESSOR is a
triplet following the pattern

#+begin_example
(NAME BIBLIOGRAPHY-STYLE CITATION-STYLE)
#+end_example

There, NAME is the name of a registered citation processor providing export
functionality, as a symbol.  BIBLIOGRAPHY-STYLE (respectively CITATION-STYLE)
is the desired default style to use when printing a bibliography (respectively
exporting a citation), as a string or nil.  Both BIBLIOGRAPHY-STYLE and
CITATION-STYLE are optional.  NAME is mandatory.

The export process selects the citation processor associated to the current
export backend, or the most specific backend the current one is derived from,
or, if all are inadequate, to the processor associated to t.  For example, with
the following value

#+begin_example
((beamer natbib)
 (latex biblatex)
 (t csl))
#+end_example

exporting with ~beamer~ or any backend derived from it will use ~natbib~,
whereas exporting with ~latex~ or any backend derived from it but different
from ~beamer~ will use ~biblatex~ processor.  Any other backend, such as
~html~, will use ~csl~ processor.

CITATION-STYLE is overridden by adding a style to any citation object.  A nil
style lets the export processor choose the default output.  Any style not
recognized by the export processor is equivalent to nil.

The citation triplet can also be set with the CITE_EXPORT keyword.
E.g.,

#+begin_example
,#+CITE_EXPORT: basic note numeric
#+end_example

or

#+begin_example
,#+CITE_EXPORT: basic
#+end_example

In that case, ~basic~ processor is used on every export, independently on the
backend.

- Default :: ~((t basic))~
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (choice
   (const :tag "No export" nil)
   (alist :key-type symbol :value-type
          (list :tag "Citation processor"
                (symbol :tag "Processor name")
                (choice
                 (const :tag "Default bibliography style" nil)
                 (string :tag "Use specific bibliography style"))
                (choice
                 (const :tag "Default citation style" nil)
                 (string :tag "Use specific citation style")))))
  #+end_src
- Since :: Org 9.5

*** org-cite-adjust-note-numbers
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-cite-adjust-note-numbers
:END:

When non-nil, allow process to modify location of note numbers.

When this variable is non-nil, it is possible to swap between author-date and
note style without modifying the document.  To that effect, citations should
always be located as in an author-date style.  Prior to turning the citation
into a footnote, the citation processor moves the citation (i.e., the future
note number), and the surrounding punctuation, according to rules defined in
~org-cite-note-rules~.

When nil, the note number is not moved.

- Default :: ~t~
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (choice
   (const :tag "Automatic note number location" t)
   (const :tag "Place note numbers manually" nil))
  #+end_src
- Since :: Org 9.5

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-cite-adjust-note-numbers = true
#+end_src

*** org-cite-note-rules
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-cite-note-rules
:END:

Alist between languages and typographic rules for citations in note style.

When ~org-cite-adjust-note-numbers~ is non-nil, and note style is requested,
citation processor is allowed to move the note marker according to some specific
rules, detailed here.  More accurately, a rule is a list following the pattern

#+begin_example
  (LANGUAGE-TAG . RULE)

LANGUAGE-TAG is a down-cased string representing a language tag as defined in
RFC 4646.  It may constituted of a language and a region separated with an
hyphen (e.g., "en-us"), or the language alone (e.g., "fr").  A language
without a region applies to all regions.

RULE is a triplet

  (PUNCTUATION NUMBER ORDER)

PUNCTUATION is the desired location of the punctuation with regards to the
quotation, if any.  It may be `inside', `outside', or `adaptive'.  The latter
permits subtler control over the punctuation: when there is no space between
the quotation mark and the punctuation, it is equivalent to `inside'.
Otherwise, it means `outside', as illustrated in the following examples:

    "A quotation ending without punctuation" [cite:@org21].
    "A quotation ending with a period"[cite:@org21].

Notwithstanding the above, a space always appear before the citation when it
is to become anything else than a note.

NUMBER is the desired location of the note number with regards to the
quotation mark, if any.  It may be `inside', `outside', or `same'.  When set
to `same', the number appears on the same side as the punctuation, unless
there is punctuation on both sides or on none.

ORDER is the relative position of the citation with regards to the closest
punctuation.  It may be `after' or `before'.
#+end_example

For example (adaptive same before) corresponds to French typography.

When the locale is unknown to this variable, the default rule is:

#+begin_example
(adaptive outside after)
#+end_example

This roughly follows the Oxford Guide to Style recommendations.

- Default ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (("en-us" inside outside after)
   ("fr" adaptive same before))
  #+end_src
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (repeat
   (list :tag "Typographic rule"
         (string :tag "Language code")
         (choice :tag "Location of punctuation"
                 (const :tag "Punctuation inside quotation" inside)
                 (const :tag "Punctuation outside quotation" outside)
                 (const :tag "Location depends on spacing" adaptive))
         (choice :tag "Location of citation"
                 (const :tag "Citation inside quotation" inside)
                 (const :tag "Citation outside quotation" outside)
                 (const :tag "Citation next to punctuation" same))
  …
  #+end_src
- Since :: Org 9.5

*** org-cite-punctuation-marks
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-cite-punctuation-marks
:END:

List of strings that can be moved around when placing note numbers.

When ~org-cite-adjust-note-numbers~ is non-nil, the citation processor is
allowed to shuffle punctuation marks specified in this list in order to
place note numbers according to rules defined in ~org-cite-note-rules~.

- Default :: ~("." "," ";" ":" "!" "?")~
- Type :: ~(repeat string)~
- Since :: Org 9.5

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-cite-punctuation-marks = [".", ",", ";", ":", "!", "?"]
#+end_src

** org-agenda.el

*** org-agenda-columns-show-summaries

*** org-agenda-columns-compute-summary-properties
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-agenda-columns-compute-summary-properties
:END:

Non-nil means recompute all summary properties before column view.
When column view in the agenda is listing properties that have a summary
operator, it can go to all relevant buffers and recompute the summaries
there.  This can mean overhead for the agenda column view, but is necessary
to have thing up to date.
As a special case, a CLOCKSUM property also makes sure that the clock
computations are current.

- Default :: ~t~
- Type :: ~boolean~

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-agenda-columns-compute-summary-properties = true
#+end_src

*** org-agenda-columns-add-appointments-to-effort-sum
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-agenda-columns-add-appointments-to-effort-sum
:END:

Non-nil means the duration of an appointment will add to day effort.
The property to which appointment durations will be added is the one given
in the option ~org-effort-property~.  If an appointment does not have
an end time, ~org-agenda-default-appointment-duration~ will be used.  If that
is not set, an appointment without end time will not contribute to the time
estimate.

- Default :: ~nil~
- Type :: ~boolean~

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-agenda-columns-add-appointments-to-effort-sum = false
#+end_src

*** org-attach-use-inheritance
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-attach-use-inheritance
:END:

Attachment inheritance for the outline.

Enabling inheritance for ~org-attach~ implies two things.  First,
that attachment links will look through all parent headings until
it finds the linked attachment.  Second, that running ~org-attach~
inside a node without attachments will make ~org-attach~ operate on
the first parent heading it finds with an attachment.

Selective means to respect the inheritance setting in
~org-use-property-inheritance~.

- Default :: ~selective~
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (choice
   (const :tag "Don't use inheritance" nil)
   (const :tag "Inherit parent node attachments" t)
   (const :tag "Respect org-use-property-inheritance" selective))
  #+end_src

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-attach-use-inheritance = "selective"
#+end_src

*** org-attach-store-link-p
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-attach-store-link-p
:END:

Non-nil means store a link to a file when attaching it.
When t, store the link to original file location.
When ~file~, store link to the attached file location.
When ~attached~, store attach: link to the attached file.

- Default :: ~attached~
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (choice
   (const :tag "Don't store link" nil)
   (const :tag "Link to origin location" t)
   (const :tag "Attachment link to the attach-dir location" attached)
   (const :tag "File link to the attach-dir location" file))
  #+end_src
- Since :: Org 9.7

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-attach-store-link-p = "attached"
#+end_src

*** org-attach-archive-delete
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-attach-archive-delete
:END:

Non-nil means attachments are deleted upon archiving a subtree.
When set to ~query~, ask the user instead.

- Default :: ~nil~
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (choice
   (const :tag "Never delete attachments" nil)
   (const :tag "Always delete attachments" t)
   (const :tag "Query the user" query))
  #+end_src
- Since :: Org 8.3

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-attach-archive-delete = false
#+end_src

*** org-attach-id-to-path-function-list
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-attach-id-to-path-function-list
:END:

List of functions used to derive attachment path from an ID string.
The functions are called with a single ID argument until the return
value is an existing folder.  If no folder has been created yet for
the given ID, then the first non-nil value defines the attachment
dir to be created.

Usually, the ID format passed to the functions is defined by
~org-id-method~.  It is advised that the first function in the list do
not generate all the attachment dirs inside the same parent dir.  Some
file systems may have performance issues in such scenario.

Care should be taken when customizing this variable.  Previously
created attachment folders might not be correctly mapped upon removing
functions from the list.  Then, Org will not be able to detect the
existing attachments.

- Default ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (org-attach-id-uuid-folder-format org-attach-id-ts-folder-format org-attach-id-fallback-folder-format)
  #+end_src
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (repeat
   (function :tag "Function with ID as input"))
  #+end_src
- Since :: Org 9.6

*** org-attach-commands
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-attach-commands
:END:

The list of commands for the attachment dispatcher.
Each entry in this list is a list of three elements:
- A list of keys (characters) to select the command (the fist
  character in the list is shown in the attachment dispatcher's
  splash buffer and minibuffer prompt).
- A command that is called interactively when one of these keys
  is pressed.
- A docstring for this command in the attachment dispatcher's
  splash buffer.

- Default ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (((97 1)
    org-attach-attach "Select a file and attach it to the task, using `org-attach-method'.")
   ((99 3)
    org-attach-attach-cp "Attach a file using copy method.")
   ((109 13)
    org-attach-attach-mv "Attach a file using move method.")
   ((108 12)
    org-attach-attach-ln "Attach a file using link method.")
   ((121 25)
    org-attach-attach-lns "Attach a file using symbolic-link method.")
   ((117 21)
    org-attach-url "Attach a file from URL (downloading it).")
   ((98)
    org-attach-buffer "Select a buffer and attach its contents to the task.")
   ((110 14)
    org-attach-new "Create a new attachment, as an Emacs buffer.")
   ((122 26)
    org-attach-sync "Synchronize the current node with its attachment\n directory, in case you added attachments yourself.\n")
   ((111 15)
  …
  #+end_src
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (repeat
   (list
    (repeat :tag "Keys" character)
    (function :tag "Command")
    (string :tag "Docstring")))
  #+end_src
- Since :: Org 9.3

*** org-attach-sync-delete-empty-dir
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-attach-sync-delete-empty-dir
:END:

Determine what to do with an empty attachment directory on sync.
When set to nil, don't touch the directory.  When set to ~query~,
ask the user instead, else remove without asking.

- Default :: ~query~
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (choice
   (const :tag "Never delete" nil)
   (const :tag "Always delete" t)
   (const :tag "Query the user" query))
  #+end_src
- Since :: Org 9.5

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-attach-sync-delete-empty-dir = "query"
#+end_src

** org-colview.el

*** org-columns-checkbox-allowed-values
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-columns-checkbox-allowed-values
:END:

Allowed values for columns with SUMMARY-TYPE that uses checkbox.
The affected summary types are "X%", "X/", and "X" (see info
node ~(org)Column attributes~).

- Default :: ~("[ ]" "[X]")~
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (repeat
   (choice
    (const :tag "Unchecked [ ]" "[ ]")
    (const :tag "Checked [X]" "[X]")
    (const :tag "No checkbox" "")
    (const :tag "Intermediate state [-]" "[-]")
    (string :tag "Arbitrary string")))
  #+end_src
- Since :: Org 9.6

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-columns-checkbox-allowed-values = ["[ ]", "[X]"]
#+end_src

*** org-columns-summary-types
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-columns-summary-types
:END:

Alist between operators and summarize functions.

Each association follows the pattern (LABEL . SUMMARIZE),
or (LABEL SUMMARIZE COLLECT) where

#+begin_example
LABEL is a string used in #+COLUMNS definition describing the
summary type.  It can contain any character but "}".  It is
case-sensitive.

SUMMARIZE is a function called with two arguments.  The first
argument is a non-empty list of values, as non-empty strings.
The second one is a format string or nil.  It has to return
a string summarizing the list of values.

COLLECT is a function called with one argument, a property
name.  It is called in the context of a headline and must
return the collected property, or the empty string.  You can
use this to only collect a property if a related conditional
properties is set, e.g., to return VACATION_DAYS only if
CONFIRMED is true.
#+end_example

Note that the return value can become one value for a higher-order
summary, so the function is expected to handle its own output.

Types defined in this variable take precedence over those defined
in ~org-columns-summary-types-default~, which see.

- Default :: ~nil~
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (alist :key-type
         (string :tag "       Label")
         :value-type
         (choice
          (function :tag "Summarize")
          (list :tag "Collect and summarize"
                (function :tag "Summarize")
                (function :tag "Collect"))))
  #+end_src
- Since :: Org 9.0

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-columns-summary-types = []
#+end_src

** org-compat.el

*** org-publish-sitemap-file-entry-format
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-publish-sitemap-file-entry-format
:END:

Format string for site-map file entry.
You could use brackets to delimit on what part the link will be.

#+begin_example
%t is the title.
%a is the author.
%d is the date.
#+end_example

- Default :: ~"%t"~
- Type :: ~string~

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-publish-sitemap-file-entry-format = "%t"
#+end_src

** org-crypt.el

*** org-crypt-tag-matcher
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-crypt-tag-matcher
:END:

The tag matcher used to find headings whose contents should be encrypted.

See the "Match syntax" section of the org manual for more details.

- Default :: ~"crypt"~
- Type :: ~string~

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-crypt-tag-matcher = "crypt"
#+end_src

*** org-crypt-key
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-crypt-key
:END:

The default key to use when encrypting the contents of a heading.

If this variable is nil, always use symmetric encryption, unconditionally.

Otherwise, The string is matched against all keys in the key ring.
In particular, the empty string matches no key.  If no key is found,
look for the ~epa-file-encrypt-to~ local variable.  Ultimately fall back
to symmetric encryption.

This setting can be overridden in the CRYPTKEY property.

- Default :: ~""~
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (choice
   (string :tag "Public key(s) matching")
   (const :tag "Symmetric encryption" nil))
  #+end_src

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-crypt-key = ""
#+end_src

*** org-crypt-disable-auto-save
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-crypt-disable-auto-save
:END:

What org-decrypt should do if ~auto-save-mode~ is enabled.

#+begin_example
t        : Disable `auto-save-mode' for the current buffer
           prior to decrypting an entry.

nil      : Leave `auto-save-mode' enabled.
           This may cause data to be written to disk unencrypted!

`ask'    : Ask user whether or not to disable `auto-save-mode'
           for the current buffer.
#+end_example

~encrypt~: Leave ~auto-save-mode~ enabled for the current buffer,

#+begin_example
but automatically re-encrypt all decrypted entries
,*before* auto-saving.
NOTE: This only works for entries which have a tag
that matches `org-crypt-tag-matcher'.
#+end_example

- Default :: ~ask~
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (choice
   (const :tag "Always" t)
   (const :tag "Never" nil)
   (const :tag "Ask" ask)
   (const :tag "Encrypt" encrypt))
  #+end_src
- Since :: Emacs 24.1

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-crypt-disable-auto-save = "ask"
#+end_src

** org-datetree.el

*** org-datetree-add-timestamp
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-datetree-add-timestamp
:END:

When non-nil, add a time stamp matching date of entry.
Added time stamp is active unless value is ~inactive~.

- Default :: ~nil~
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (choice
   (const :tag "Do not add a time stamp" nil)
   (const :tag "Add an inactive time stamp" inactive)
   (const :tag "Add an active time stamp" active))
  #+end_src
- Since :: Emacs 24.3

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-datetree-add-timestamp = false
#+end_src

** org-footnote.el

*** org-footnote-section
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-footnote-section
:END:

Outline heading containing footnote definitions.

This can be nil, to place footnotes locally at the end of the current
outline node.  It can also be a string representing the name of a
special outline heading under which footnotes should be put.

This variable defines the place where Org puts the definition
automatically, i.e. when creating the footnote, and when sorting
the notes.  However, by hand, you may place definitions

#+begin_example
,*anywhere*.
#+end_example

If this is a string, during export, all subtrees starting with
this heading will be ignored.

If you don't use the customize interface to change this variable,
you will need to run the following command after the change:

#+begin_example
`C-u M-x org-element-cache-reset'
#+end_example

- Default :: ~"Footnotes"~
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (choice
   (string :tag "Collect footnotes under heading")
   (const :tag "Define footnotes locally" nil))
  #+end_src

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-footnote-section = "Footnotes"
#+end_src

*** org-footnote-define-inline
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-footnote-define-inline
:END:

Non-nil means define footnotes inline, at reference location.
When nil, footnotes will be defined in a special section near
the end of the document.  When t, the ~[fn:label:definition]~ notation
will be used to define the footnote at the reference position.

- Default :: ~nil~
- Type :: ~boolean~

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-footnote-define-inline = false
#+end_src

*** org-footnote-auto-label
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-footnote-auto-label
:END:

Non-nil means define automatically new labels for footnotes.
Possible values are:

#+begin_example
nil        Prompt the user for each label.
t          Create unique labels of the form [fn:1], [fn:2], etc.
anonymous  Create anonymous footnotes
confirm    Like t, but let the user edit the created value.
           The label can be removed from the minibuffer to create
           an anonymous footnote.
random           Automatically generate a unique, random label.
#+end_example

- Default :: ~t~
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (choice
   (const :tag "Prompt for label" nil)
   (const :tag "Create automatic [fn:N]" t)
   (const :tag "Offer automatic [fn:N] for editing" confirm)
   (const :tag "Create anonymous [fn::]" anonymous)
   (const :tag "Create a random label" random))
  #+end_src
- Since :: Org 9.7

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-footnote-auto-label = true
#+end_src

*** org-footnote-auto-adjust
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-footnote-auto-adjust
:END:

Non-nil means automatically adjust footnotes after insert/delete.
When this is t, after each insertion or deletion of a footnote,
simple fn:N footnotes will be renumbered, and all footnotes will be sorted.
If you want to have just sorting or just renumbering, set this variable
to ~sort~ or ~renumber~.

The main values of this variable can be set with in-buffer options:

#+begin_example
,#+STARTUP: fnadjust
,#+STARTUP: nofnadjust
#+end_example

- Default :: ~nil~
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (choice
   (const :tag "No adjustment" nil)
   (const :tag "Renumber" renumber)
   (const :tag "Sort" sort)
   (const :tag "Renumber and Sort" t))
  #+end_src

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-footnote-auto-adjust = false
#+end_src

*** org-footnote-fill-after-inline-note-extraction
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-footnote-fill-after-inline-note-extraction
:END:

Non-nil means fill paragraphs after extracting footnotes.
When extracting inline footnotes, the lengths of lines can change a lot.
When this option is set, paragraphs from which an inline footnote has been
extracted will be filled again.

- Default :: ~nil~
- Type :: ~boolean~

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-footnote-fill-after-inline-note-extraction = false
#+end_src

** org-plot.el

*** org-plot/gnuplot-script-preamble
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-plotgnuplot-script-preamble
:END:

String of function to be inserted before the gnuplot plot command is run.

Note that this is in addition to, not instead of other content generated
in ~org-plot/gnuplot-script~.  If a function, it is called with the
parameters used by the current plot type (see
~org-plot/preset-plot-types~), and must return a string to be used.

- Default :: ~""~
- Type :: ~(choice string function)~

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
"org-plot/gnuplot-script-preamble" = ""
#+end_src

*** org-plot/preset-plot-types
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-plotpreset-plot-types
:END:

List of plists describing the available plot types.
The car is the type name, and the property :plot-func must be
set.  The value of :plot-func is a lambda which yields plot-lines
(a list of strings) as the cdr.

All lambda functions have the parameters of
~org-plot/gnuplot-script~ and PLOT-STR passed to them.  i.e. they
are called with the following signature: (TABLE DATA-FILE
NUM-COLS PARAMS PLOT-STR)

Potentially useful parameters in PARAMS include:

#+begin_example
:set :line :map :title :file :ind :timeind :timefmt :textind
:deps :labels :xlabels :ylabels :xmin :xmax :ymin :ymax :ticks
#+end_example

In addition to :plot-func, the following optional properties may
be set.

- :plot-cmd - A gnuplot command appended to each plot-line.
  Accepts string or nil.  Default value: nil.

- :check-ind-type - Whether the types of ind values should be checked.
  Accepts boolean.

- :plot-str - the formula string passed to :plot-func as PLOT-STR
  Accepts string.  Default value: "'%s' using %s%d%s with %s title '%s'"

- :data-dump - Function to dump the table to a datafile for ease of
  use.

#+begin_example
Accepts function with arguments:
(table data-file num-cols params)
#+end_example

- :plot-pre - Gnuplot code to be inserted early into the script, just
  after term and output have been set.

#+begin_example
Accepts string, nil, or lambda function which returns string
or nil.  Defaults to nil.
#+end_example

- Default ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  ((2d :plot-cmd "plot" :check-ind-type t :plot-func
       (lambda
         (_table data-file num-cols params plot-str)
         (let*
             ((type
               (plist-get params :plot-type))
              (with
               (if
                   (eq type 'grid)
                   'pm3d
                 (plist-get params :with)))
              (ind
               (plist-get params :ind))
              (deps
               (if
                   (plist-member params :deps)
                   (plist-get params :deps)))
              (text-ind
               (or
  …
  #+end_src
- Type :: ~alist~
- Since :: Org 9.8

*** org-plot/gnuplot-term-extra
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-plotgnuplot-term-extra
:END:

String or function which provides the extra term options.
E.g. a value of "size 1050,650" would cause
"set term ... size 1050,650" to be used.
If a function, it is called with the parameters used by the current plot
type, see ~org-plot/preset-plot-types~.

- Default :: ~""~
- Type :: ~(choice string function)~

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
"org-plot/gnuplot-term-extra" = ""
#+end_src

** org-src.el

*** org-edit-src-turn-on-auto-save
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-edit-src-turn-on-auto-save
:END:

Non-nil means turn ~auto-save-mode~ on when editing a source block.
This will save the content of the source code editing buffer into
a newly created file, not the base buffer for this source block.

If you want to regularly save the base buffer instead of the source
code editing buffer, see ~org-edit-src-auto-save-idle-delay~ instead.

- Default :: ~nil~
- Type :: ~boolean~
- Since :: Org 8.0

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-edit-src-turn-on-auto-save = false
#+end_src

*** org-edit-src-auto-save-idle-delay
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-edit-src-auto-save-idle-delay
:END:

Delay before saving a source code buffer back into its base buffer.
When a positive integer N, save after N seconds of idle time.
When 0 (the default), don't auto-save.

If you want to save the source code buffer itself, don't use this.
Check ~org-edit-src-turn-on-auto-save~ instead.

- Default :: ~0~
- Type :: ~integer~
- Since :: Org 8.0

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-edit-src-auto-save-idle-delay = 0
#+end_src

*** org-coderef-label-format
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-coderef-label-format
:END:

The default coderef format.
This format string will be used to search for coderef labels in literal
examples (EXAMPLE and SRC blocks).  The format can be overwritten in
an individual literal example with the -l option, like

#+begin_example
,#+BEGIN_SRC pascal +n -r -l "((%s))"
...
,#+END_SRC
#+end_example

If you want to use this for HTML export, make sure that the format does
not introduce special font-locking, and avoid the HTML special
characters ~<~, ~>~, and ~&~.  The reason for this restriction is that
the labels are searched for only after htmlize has done its job.

- Default :: ~"(ref:%s)"~
- Type :: ~string~

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-coderef-label-format = "(ref:%s)"
#+end_src

*** org-edit-fixed-width-region-mode
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-edit-fixed-width-region-mode
:END:

The mode that should be used to edit fixed-width regions.
These are the regions where each line starts with a colon.

- Default :: ~artist-mode~
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (choice
   (const artist-mode)
   (const picture-mode)
   (const fundamental-mode)
   (function :tag "Other (specify)"))
  #+end_src

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-edit-fixed-width-region-mode = "artist-mode"
#+end_src

*** org-edit-src-persistent-message
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-edit-src-persistent-message
:END:

Non-nil means show persistent exit help message while editing src examples.
The message is shown in the header-line, which will be created in the
first line of the window showing the editing buffer.

- Default :: ~t~
- Type :: ~boolean~

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-edit-src-persistent-message = true
#+end_src

*** org-src-ask-before-returning-to-edit-buffer
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-src-ask-before-returning-to-edit-buffer
:END:

Non-nil means ask before switching to an existing edit buffer.
If nil, when ~org-edit-src-code~ is used on a block that already
has an active edit buffer, it will switch to that edit buffer
immediately; otherwise it will ask whether you want to return to
the existing edit buffer.

- Default :: ~t~
- Type :: ~boolean~
- Since :: Org 8.0

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-src-ask-before-returning-to-edit-buffer = true
#+end_src

*** org-src-window-setup
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-src-window-setup
:END:

How the source code edit buffer should be displayed.
Possible values for this option are:

#+begin_example
plain              Show edit buffer using `display-buffer'.  Users can
                   further control the display behavior by modifying
                   `display-buffer-alist' and its relatives.
current-window     Show edit buffer in the current window, keeping all other
                   windows.
#+end_example

split-window-below Show edit buffer below the current window, keeping all

#+begin_example
other windows.
#+end_example

split-window-right Show edit buffer to the right of the current window,

#+begin_example
                   keeping all other windows.
other-window       Use `switch-to-buffer-other-window' to display edit buffer.
reorganize-frame   Show only two windows on the current frame, the current
                   window and the edit buffer.
other-frame        Use `switch-to-buffer-other-frame' to display edit buffer.
                   Also, when exiting the edit buffer, kill that frame.
#+end_example

Values that modify the window layout (reorganize-frame, split-window-below,
split-window-right) will restore the layout after exiting the edit buffer.

- Default :: ~reorganize-frame~
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (choice
   (const plain)
   (const current-window)
   (const split-window-below)
   (const split-window-right)
   (const other-frame)
   (const other-window)
   (const reorganize-frame))
  #+end_src

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-src-window-setup = "reorganize-frame"
#+end_src

*** org-src-lang-modes
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-src-lang-modes
:END:

Alist mapping languages to their major mode.

The key is the language name.  The value is the mode name, as
a string or a symbol, without the "-mode" suffix.

For many languages this is simple, but for language where this is
not the case, this variable provides a way to simplify things on
the user side.  For example, there is no ~ocaml-mode~ in Emacs,
but the mode to use is ~tuareg-mode~.

- Default :: computed when Org loads:
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  `(("C" . c)
    ("C++" . c++)
    ("asymptote" . asy)
    ("beamer" . latex)
    ("calc" . fundamental)
    ("cpp" . c++)
    ("ditaa" . artist)
    ("desktop" . conf-desktop)
    ("dot" . graphviz-dot)
    ("elisp" . emacs-lisp)
    ("ocaml" . tuareg)
    ("screen" . shell-script)
    ("sqlite" . sql)
    ("toml" . conf-toml)
    ("shell" . sh)
    ,@(org-src--get-known-shells))
  #+end_src
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (repeat
   (cons
    (string :tag "Language name")
    (symbol :tag "Major mode")))
  #+end_src
- Since :: Org 9.8

*** org-babel-load-languages
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-babel-load-languages
:END:

Languages which can be evaluated in Org buffers.

This list can be used to load support for any of the available
languages with babel support (see info node ~(org) Languages~).  Each
language will depend on a different set of system executables and/or
Emacs modes.

When a language is "loaded", code blocks in that language can
be evaluated with ~org-babel-execute-src-block~, which is bound
by default to C-c C-c.

The ~org-babel-no-eval-on-ctrl-c-ctrl-c~ option can be set to
remove code block evaluation from C-c C-c.  By
default, only Emacs Lisp is loaded, since it has no specific
requirement.

- Default :: ~((emacs-lisp . t))~
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (alist :tag "Babel Languages" :key-type
         (choice
          (const :tag "Awk" awk)
          (const :tag "C, D, C++, and cpp" C)
          (const :tag "R" R)
          (const :tag "Calc" calc)
          (const :tag "Clojure and ClojureScript" clojure)
          (const :tag "CSS" css)
          (const :tag "Ditaa" ditaa)
          (const :tag "Dot" dot)
          (const :tag "Emacs Lisp" emacs-lisp)
  …
  #+end_src
- Since :: Org 9.6

org-babel-load-languages = { emacs-lisp = true }

Remove ID property of clones of a subtree.

#+end_src

*** org-export-backends
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-backends
:END:

List of export backends that should be always available.

If a description starts with <C>, the file is not part of Emacs and Org mode,
so loading it will require that you have properly installed org-contrib
package from NonGNU Emacs Lisp Package Archive
https://elpa.nongnu.org/nongnu/org-contrib.html

Unlike to ~org-modules~, libraries in this list will not be
loaded along with Org, but only once the export framework is
needed.

This variable needs to be set before org.el is loaded.  If you
need to make a change while Emacs is running, use the customize
interface or run the following code, where VAL stands for the new
value of the variable, after updating it:

#+begin_example
(progn
  (setq org-export-registered-backends
        (cl-remove-if-not
         (lambda (backend)
           (let ((name (org-export-backend-name backend)))
             (or (memq name val)
                 (catch 'parentp
                   (dolist (b val)
                     (and (org-export-derived-backend-p b name)
                          (throw 'parentp t)))))))
         org-export-registered-backends))
  (let ((new-list (mapcar #'org-export-backend-name
                          org-export-registered-backends)))
    (dolist (backend val)
      (cond
       ((not (load (format "ox-%s" backend) t t))
        (message "Problems while trying to load export backend `%s'"
                 backend))
       ((not (memq backend new-list)) (push backend new-list))))
    (set-default 'org-export-backends new-list)))
#+end_example

Adding a backend to this list will also pull the backend it
depends on, if any.

- Default :: ~(ascii html icalendar latex odt)~
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (set :greedy t
       (const :tag "   ascii       Export buffer to ASCII format" ascii)
       (const :tag "   beamer      Export buffer to Beamer presentation" beamer)
       (const :tag "   html        Export buffer to HTML format" html)
       (const :tag "   icalendar   Export buffer to iCalendar format" icalendar)
       (const :tag "   latex       Export buffer to LaTeX format" latex)
       (const :tag "   man         Export buffer to MAN format" man)
       (const :tag "   md          Export buffer to Markdown format" md)
       (const :tag "   odt         Export buffer to ODT format" odt)
       (const :tag "   org         Export buffer to Org format" org)
       (const :tag "   texinfo     Export buffer to Texinfo format" texinfo)
  …
  #+end_src
- Since :: Org 9.0

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-export-backends = ["ascii", "html", "icalendar", "latex", "odt"]
#+end_src

This will be used for column view in the agenda unless a format has
