#+end_src

*** org-odt-create-custom-styles-for-srcblocks
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-odt-create-custom-styles-for-srcblocks
:END:

Whether custom styles for colorized source blocks be automatically created.
When this option is turned on, the exporter creates custom styles
for source blocks based on the advice of ~htmlfontify~.  Creation
of custom styles happen as part of ~org-odt-hfy-face-to-css~.

When this option is turned off exporter does not create such
styles.

Use the latter option if you do not want the custom styles to be
based on your current display settings.  It is necessary that the
styles.xml already contains needed styles for colorizing to work.

This variable is effective only if ~org-odt-fontify-srcblocks~ is
turned on.

- Default :: ~t~
- Type :: ~boolean~
- Since :: Emacs 24.1

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-odt-create-custom-styles-for-srcblocks = true
#+end_src

*** org-odt-fontify-srcblocks
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-odt-fontify-srcblocks
:END:

Specify whether or not source blocks need to be fontified.
Turn this option on if you want to colorize the source code
blocks in the exported file.  For colorization to work, you need
to make available an enhanced version of ~htmlfontify~ library.

- Default :: ~t~
- Type :: ~boolean~
- Since :: Emacs 24.1

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-odt-fontify-srcblocks = true
#+end_src

*** org-odt-table-styles
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-odt-table-styles
:END:

Specify how Table Styles should be derived from a Table Template.
This is a list where each element is of the
form (TABLE-STYLE-NAME TABLE-TEMPLATE-NAME TABLE-CELL-OPTIONS).

TABLE-STYLE-NAME is the style associated with the table through
"#+ATTR_ODT: :style TABLE-STYLE-NAME" line.

TABLE-TEMPLATE-NAME is a set of - up to 9 - automatic
TABLE-CELL-STYLE-NAMEs and PARAGRAPH-STYLE-NAMEs (as defined
below) that is included in ~org-odt-content-template-file~.

TABLE-CELL-STYLE-NAME := TABLE-TEMPLATE-NAME + TABLE-CELL-TYPE +

#+begin_example
                         "TableCell"
PARAGRAPH-STYLE-NAME  := TABLE-TEMPLATE-NAME + TABLE-CELL-TYPE +
                         "TableParagraph"
TABLE-CELL-TYPE       := "FirstRow"   | "LastColumn" |
                         "FirstRow"   | "LastRow"    |
                         "EvenRow"    | "OddRow"     |
                         "EvenColumn" | "OddColumn"  | ""
#+end_example

where "+" above denotes string concatenation.

TABLE-CELL-OPTIONS is an alist where each element is of the
form (TABLE-CELL-STYLE-SELECTOR . ON-OR-OFF).
TABLE-CELL-STYLE-SELECTOR := ~use-first-row-styles~       |

#+begin_example
                             `use-last-row-styles'        |
                             `use-first-column-styles'    |
                             `use-last-column-styles'     |
                             `use-banding-rows-styles'    |
                             `use-banding-columns-styles' |
                             `use-first-row-styles'
ON-OR-OFF                 := t | nil
#+end_example

For example, with the following configuration

(setq org-odt-table-styles

#+begin_example
'(("TableWithHeaderRowsAndColumns" "Custom"
   ((use-first-row-styles . t)
    (use-first-column-styles . t)))
  ("TableWithHeaderColumns" "Custom"
   ((use-first-column-styles . t)))))
#+end_example

1. A table associated with "TableWithHeaderRowsAndColumns"

#+begin_example
style will use the following table-cell styles -
"CustomFirstRowTableCell", "CustomFirstColumnTableCell",
"CustomTableCell" and the following paragraph styles
"CustomFirstRowTableParagraph",
"CustomFirstColumnTableParagraph", "CustomTableParagraph"
as appropriate.
#+end_example

2. A table associated with "TableWithHeaderColumns" style will

#+begin_example
use the following table-cell styles -
"CustomFirstColumnTableCell", "CustomTableCell" and the
following paragraph styles
"CustomFirstColumnTableParagraph", "CustomTableParagraph"
as appropriate..
#+end_example

Note that TABLE-TEMPLATE-NAME corresponds to the
"<table:table-template>" elements contained within
"<office:styles>".  The entries (TABLE-STYLE-NAME
TABLE-TEMPLATE-NAME TABLE-CELL-OPTIONS) correspond to
"table:template-name" and "table:use-first-row-styles" etc
attributes of "<table:table>" element.  Refer ODF-1.2
specification for more information.  Also consult the
implementation filed under ~org-odt-get-table-cell-styles~.

The TABLE-STYLE-NAME "OrgEquation" is used internally for
formatting of numbered display equations.  Do not delete this
style from the list.

- Default ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (("OrgEquation" "OrgEquation"
    ((use-first-column-styles . t)
     (use-last-column-styles . t)))
   ("TableWithHeaderRowAndColumn" "Custom"
    ((use-first-row-styles . t)
     (use-first-column-styles . t)))
   ("TableWithFirstRowandLastRow" "Custom"
    ((use-first-row-styles . t)
     (use-last-row-styles . t)))
   ("GriddedTable" "Custom" nil))
  #+end_src
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (choice
   (const :tag "None" nil)
   (repeat :tag "Table Styles"
           (list :tag "Table Style Specification"
                 (string :tag "Table Style Name")
                 (string :tag "Table Template Name")
                 (alist :options
                        (use-first-row-styles use-last-row-styles use-first-column-styles use-last-column-styles use-banding-rows-styles use-banding-columns-styles)
                        :key-type symbol :value-type
                        (const :tag "True" t)))))
  #+end_src
- Since :: Emacs 24.1

*** org-odt-use-date-fields
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-odt-use-date-fields
:END:

Non-nil, if timestamps should be exported as date fields.

When nil, export timestamps as plain text.

When non-nil, map ~org-timestamp-custom-formats~ to a pair of
OpenDocument date-styles with names "OrgDate1" and "OrgDate2"
respectively.  A timestamp with no time component is formatted
with style "OrgDate1" while one with explicit hour and minutes
is formatted with style "OrgDate2".

This feature is experimental.  Most (but not all) of the common

#+begin_example
%-specifiers in `format-time-string' are supported.
#+end_example

Specifically, locale-dependent specifiers like "%c", "%x" are
formatted as canonical Org timestamps.  For finer control, avoid
these %-specifiers.

Textual specifiers like "%b", "%h", "%B", "%a", "%A"
etc., are displayed by the application in the default language
and country specified in ~org-odt-styles-file~.  Note that the
default styles file uses language "en" and country "GB".  You
can localize the week day and month strings in the exported
document by setting the default language and country either using
the application UI or through a custom styles file.

See ~org-odt--build-date-styles~ for implementation details.

- Default :: ~nil~
- Type :: ~boolean~
- Since :: Org 8.0

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-odt-use-date-fields = false
#+end_src

** ox-org.el

*** org-org-htmlized-css-url
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-org-htmlized-css-url
:END:

URL pointing to the CSS defining colors for htmlized Emacs buffers.
Normally when creating an htmlized version of an Org buffer,
htmlize will create the CSS to define the font colors.  However,
this does not work when converting in batch mode, and it also can
look bad if different people with different fontification setup
work on the same website.  When this variable is non-nil,
creating an htmlized version of an Org buffer using
~org-org-export-as-org~ will include a link to this URL if the
setting of ~org-html-htmlize-output-type~ is ~css~.

- Default :: ~nil~
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (choice
   (const :tag "Don't include external stylesheet link" nil)
   (string :tag "URL or local href"))
  #+end_src

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-org-htmlized-css-url = false
#+end_src

*** org-org-with-special-rows
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-org-with-special-rows
:END:

Non-nil means export special table rows.
Special rows are the rows containing special marking characters, as
described in the Info node ~(org)Advanced features~.

- Default :: ~t~
- Type :: ~boolean~
- Since :: Org 9.7

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-org-with-special-rows = true
#+end_src

*** org-org-with-cite-processors
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-org-with-cite-processors
:END:

Non-nil means use citation processors when exporting citations.

- Default :: ~nil~
- Type :: ~boolean~
- Since :: Org 9.7

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-org-with-cite-processors = false
#+end_src

** ox-publish.el

*** org-publish-project-alist
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-publish-project-alist
:END:

Association list to control publishing behavior.

Each element of the alist is a publishing project.  The car of
each element is a string, uniquely identifying the project.  The
cdr of each element is in one of the following forms:

1. A well-formed property list with an even number of elements,

#+begin_example
alternating keys and values, specifying parameters for the
publishing process.

  (:property value :property value ... )
#+end_example

2. A meta-project definition, specifying of a list of

#+begin_example
sub-projects:

  (:components ("project-1" "project-2" ...))
#+end_example

When the CDR of an element of ~org-publish-project-alist~ is in
this second form, the elements of the list after ~:components~
are taken to be components of the project, which group together
files requiring different publishing options.  When you publish
such a project with ~M-x org-publish~, the components all publish.

When a property is given a value in ~org-publish-project-alist~,
its setting overrides the value of the corresponding user
variable (if any) during publishing.  However, options set within
a file override everything.

Most properties are optional, but some should always be set:

#+begin_example
`:base-directory'

  Directory containing publishing source files.

`:base-extension'

  Extension (without the dot!) of source files.  This can be
  a regular expression.  If not given, "org" will be used as
  default extension.  If it is `any', include all the files,
  even without extension.

`:publishing-directory'

  Directory (possibly remote) where output files will be
  published.
#+end_example

If ~:recursive~ is non-nil files in sub-directories of
~:base-directory~ are considered.

The ~:exclude~ property may be used to prevent certain files from
being published.  Its value may be a string or regexp matching
file names you don't want to be published.

The ~:include~ property may be used to include extra files.  Its
value may be a list of filenames to include.  The filenames are
considered relative to the base directory.

When both ~:include~ and ~:exclude~ properties are given values,
the exclusion step happens first.

One special property controls which backend function to use for
publishing files in the project.  This can be used to extend the
set of file types publishable by ~org-publish~, as well as the
set of output formats.

#+begin_example
`:publishing-function'

  Function to publish file.  Each backend may define its
  own (i.e. `org-latex-publish-to-pdf',
  `org-html-publish-to-html').  May be a list of functions, in
  which case each function in the list is invoked in turn.
#+end_example

Another property allows you to insert code that prepares
a project for publishing.  For example, you could call GNU Make
on a certain makefile, to ensure published files are built up to
date.

#+begin_example
`:preparation-function'

  Function to be called before publishing this project.  This
  may also be a list of functions.  Preparation functions are
  called with the project properties list as their sole
  argument.

`:completion-function'

  Function to be called after publishing this project.  This
  may also be a list of functions.  Completion functions are
  called with the project properties list as their sole
  argument.
#+end_example

Some properties control details of the Org publishing process,
and are equivalent to the corresponding user variables listed in
the right column.  Backend specific properties may also be
included.  See the backend documentation for more information.

#+begin_example
:author                   variable `user-full-name'
:creator                  `org-export-creator-string'
:email                    `user-mail-address'
:exclude-tags             `org-export-exclude-tags'
:headline-levels          `org-export-headline-levels'
:language                 `org-export-default-language'
:preserve-breaks          `org-export-preserve-breaks'
:section-numbers          `org-export-with-section-numbers'
:select-tags              `org-export-select-tags'
:time-stamp-file          `org-export-timestamp-file'
:with-archived-trees      `org-export-with-archived-trees'
:with-author              `org-export-with-author'
:with-creator             `org-export-with-creator'
:with-date                `org-export-with-date'
:with-drawers             `org-export-with-drawers'
:with-email               `org-export-with-email'
:with-emphasize           `org-export-with-emphasize'
:with-entities            `org-export-with-entities'
:with-fixed-width         `org-export-with-fixed-width'
:with-footnotes           `org-export-with-footnotes'
:with-inlinetasks         `org-export-with-inlinetasks'
:with-latex               `org-export-with-latex'
:with-planning            `org-export-with-planning'
:with-priority            `org-export-with-priority'
:with-properties          `org-export-with-properties'
:with-smart-quotes        `org-export-with-smart-quotes'
:with-special-strings     `org-export-with-special-strings'
:with-statistics-cookies' `org-export-with-statistics-cookies'
:with-sub-superscript     `org-export-with-sub-superscripts'
:with-toc                 `org-export-with-toc'
:with-tables              `org-export-with-tables'
:with-tags                `org-export-with-tags'
:with-tasks               `org-export-with-tasks'
:with-timestamps          `org-export-with-timestamps'
:with-title               `org-export-with-title'
:with-todo-keywords       `org-export-with-todo-keywords'
#+end_example

The following properties may be used to control publishing of
a site-map of files or summary page for a given project.

#+begin_example
`:auto-sitemap'

  Whether to publish a site-map during
  `org-publish-current-project' or `org-publish-all'.

`:sitemap-filename'

  Filename for output of site-map.  Defaults to "sitemap.org".

`:sitemap-title'

  Title of site-map page.  Defaults to name of file.

`:sitemap-style'

  Can be `list' (site-map is just an itemized list of the
  titles of the files involved) or `tree' (the directory
  structure of the source files is reflected in the site-map).
  Defaults to `tree'.

`:sitemap-format-entry'

  Plugin function used to format entries in the site-map.  It
  is called with three arguments: the file or directory name
  relative to base directory, the site map style and the
  current project.  It has to return a string.

  Defaults to `org-publish-sitemap-default-entry', which turns
  file names into links and use document titles as
  descriptions.  For specific formatting needs, one can use
  `org-publish-find-date', `org-publish-find-title' and
  `org-publish-find-property', to retrieve additional
  information about published documents.

`:sitemap-function'

  Plugin function to use for generation of site-map.  It is
  called with two arguments: the title of the site-map, as
  a string, and a representation of the files involved in the
  project, as returned by `org-list-to-lisp'.  The latter can
  further be transformed using `org-list-to-generic',
  `org-list-to-subtree' and alike.  It has to return a string.

  Defaults to `org-publish-sitemap-default', which generates
  a plain list of links to all files in the project.
#+end_example

If you create a site-map file, adjust the sorting like this:

#+begin_example
`:sitemap-sort-folders'

  Where folders should appear in the site-map.  Set this to
  `first' or `last' to display folders first or last,
  respectively.  When set to `ignore' (default), folders are
  ignored altogether.  Any other value will mix files and
  folders.  This variable has no effect when site-map style is
  `tree'.

`:sitemap-sort-files'

  The site map is normally sorted alphabetically.  You can
  change this behavior setting this to `anti-chronologically',
  `chronologically', or nil.

`:sitemap-ignore-case'

  Should sorting be case-sensitive?  Default nil.
#+end_example

The following property control the creation of a concept index.

#+begin_example
`:makeindex'

  Create a concept index.  The file containing the index has to
  be called "theindex.org".  If it doesn't exist in the
  project, it will be generated.  Contents of the index are
  stored in the file "theindex.inc", which can be included in
  "theindex.org".
#+end_example

Other properties affecting publication.

#+begin_example
`:body-only'

  Set this to t to publish only the body of the documents.
#+end_example

- Default :: ~nil~
- Type :: ~alist~

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-publish-project-alist = []
#+end_src

*** org-publish-use-timestamps-flag
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-publish-use-timestamps-flag
:END:

Non-nil means use timestamp checking to publish only changed files.
When nil, do no timestamp checking and always publish all files.

- Default :: ~t~
- Type :: ~boolean~

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-publish-use-timestamps-flag = true
#+end_src

*** org-publish-timestamp-directory
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-publish-timestamp-directory
:END:

Name of directory in which to store publishing timestamps.

- Default :: computed when Org loads: ~(convert-standard-filename "~/.org-timestamps/")~
- Type :: ~directory~

*** org-publish-list-skipped-files
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-publish-list-skipped-files
:END:

Non-nil means show message about files ~*not*~ published.

- Default :: ~t~
- Type :: ~boolean~

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-publish-list-skipped-files = true
#+end_src

*** org-publish-sitemap-sort-files
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-publish-sitemap-sort-files
:END:

Method to sort files in site-maps.
Possible values are ~alphabetically~, ~chronologically~,
~anti-chronologically~ and nil.

If ~alphabetically~, files will be sorted alphabetically.  If
~chronologically~, files will be sorted with older modification
time first.  If ~anti-chronologically~, files will be sorted with
newer modification time first.  nil won't sort files.

You can overwrite this default per project in your
~org-publish-project-alist~, using ~:sitemap-sort-files~.

- Default :: ~alphabetically~
- Type :: ~symbol~

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-publish-sitemap-sort-files = "alphabetically"
#+end_src

*** org-publish-sitemap-sort-folders
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-publish-sitemap-sort-folders
:END:

A symbol, denoting if folders are sorted first in site-maps.

Possible values are ~first~, ~last~, ~ignore~ and nil.
If ~first~, folders will be sorted before files.
If ~last~, folders are sorted to the end after the files.
If ~ignore~, folders do not appear in the site-map.
Any other value will mix files and folders.

You can overwrite this default per project in your
~org-publish-project-alist~, using ~:sitemap-sort-folders~.

This variable is ignored when site-map style is ~tree~.

- Default :: ~ignore~
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (choice
   (const :tag "Folders before files" first)
   (const :tag "Folders after files" last)
   (const :tag "No folder in site-map" ignore)
   (const :tag "Mix folders and files" nil))
  #+end_src
- Since :: Org 9.1

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-publish-sitemap-sort-folders = "ignore"
#+end_src

*** org-publish-sitemap-sort-ignore-case
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-publish-sitemap-sort-ignore-case
:END:

Non-nil when site-map sorting should ignore case.

You can overwrite this default per project in your
~org-publish-project-alist~, using ~:sitemap-ignore-case~.

- Default :: ~nil~
- Type :: ~boolean~

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-publish-sitemap-sort-ignore-case = false
#+end_src

** ox-texinfo.el

*** org-texinfo-coding-system
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-texinfo-coding-system
:END:

Default document encoding for Texinfo output.

If nil it will default to ~buffer-file-coding-system~.

- Default :: ~nil~
- Type :: ~coding-system~

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-texinfo-coding-system = false
#+end_src

*** org-texinfo-default-class
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-texinfo-default-class
:END:

The default Texinfo class.

- Default :: ~"info"~
- Type :: ~(string :tag "Texinfo class")~

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-texinfo-default-class = "info"
#+end_src

*** org-texinfo-classes
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-texinfo-classes
:END:

Alist of Texinfo classes and associated header and structure.
If #+TEXINFO_CLASS is set in the buffer, use its value and the
associated information.  Here is the structure of a class
definition:

#+begin_example
(class-name
  header-string
  (numbered-1 unnumbered-1 unnumbered-no-toc-1 appendix-1)
  (numbered-2 unnumbered-2 unnumbered-no-toc-2 appendix-2)
  ...)
#+end_example

The header string

#+begin_example
-----------------
#+end_example

The header string is inserted in the header of the generated
document, right after "@setfilename" and "@settitle"
commands.

If it contains the special string

#+begin_example
"@documentencoding AUTO"
#+end_example

"AUTO" will be replaced with an appropriate coding system.  See
~org-texinfo-coding-system~ for more information.  Likewise, if
the string contains the special string

#+begin_example
"@documentlanguage AUTO"
#+end_example

"AUTO" will be replaced with the language defined in the
buffer, through #+LANGUAGE keyword, or globally, with
~org-export-default-language~, which see.

The sectioning structure

#+begin_example
------------------------
#+end_example

The sectioning structure of the class is given by the elements
following the header string.  For each sectioning level, a number
of strings is specified.  A %s formatter is mandatory in each
section string and will be replaced by the title of the section.

- Default ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (("info" "@documentencoding AUTO\n@documentlanguage AUTO"
    ("@chapter %s" "@unnumbered %s" "@chapheading %s" "@appendix %s")
    ("@section %s" "@unnumberedsec %s" "@heading %s" "@appendixsec %s")
    ("@subsection %s" "@unnumberedsubsec %s" "@subheading %s" "@appendixsubsec %s")
    ("@subsubsection %s" "@unnumberedsubsubsec %s" "@subsubheading %s" "@appendixsubsubsec %s")))
  #+end_src
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (repeat
   (list
    (string :tag "Texinfo class")
    (string :tag "Texinfo header")
    (repeat :tag "Levels" :inline t
            (choice
             (list :tag "Heading"
                   (string :tag "         numbered")
                   (string :tag "       unnumbered")
                   (string :tag "unnumbered-no-toc")
                   (string :tag "         appendix"))))))
  #+end_src
- Since :: Org 9.2

*** org-texinfo-format-headline-function
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-texinfo-format-headline-function
:END:

Function to format headline text.

This function will be called with 5 arguments:

#+begin_example
TODO      the todo keyword (string or nil).
#+end_example

TODO-TYPE the type of todo (symbol: ~todo~, ~done~, nil)

#+begin_example
PRIORITY  the priority of the headline (integer or nil)
TEXT      the main headline text (string).
TAGS      the tags as a list of strings (list of strings or nil).
#+end_example

The function result will be used in the section format string.

- Default :: ~org-texinfo-format-headline-default-function~
- Type :: ~function~
- Since :: Org 8.3

*** org-texinfo-node-description-column
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-texinfo-node-description-column
:END:

Column at which to start the description in the node listings.
If a node title is greater than this length, the description will
be placed after the end of the title.

- Default :: ~32~
- Type :: ~integer~

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-texinfo-node-description-column = 32
#+end_src

*** org-texinfo-active-timestamp-format
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-texinfo-active-timestamp-format
:END:

A ~format~ string to be applied to active timestamps.

- Default :: ~"@emph{%s}"~
- Type :: ~string~

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-texinfo-active-timestamp-format = "@emph{%s}"
#+end_src

*** org-texinfo-inactive-timestamp-format
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-texinfo-inactive-timestamp-format
:END:

A ~format~ string to be applied to inactive timestamps.

- Default :: ~"@emph{%s}"~
- Type :: ~string~

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-texinfo-inactive-timestamp-format = "@emph{%s}"
#+end_src

*** org-texinfo-diary-timestamp-format
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-texinfo-diary-timestamp-format
:END:

A ~format~ string to be applied to diary timestamps.

- Default :: ~"@emph{%s}"~
- Type :: ~string~

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-texinfo-diary-timestamp-format = "@emph{%s}"
#+end_src

*** org-texinfo-link-with-unknown-path-format
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-texinfo-link-with-unknown-path-format
:END:

Format string for links with unknown path type.

- Default :: ~"@indicateurl{%s}"~
- Type :: ~string~

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-texinfo-link-with-unknown-path-format = "@indicateurl{%s}"
#+end_src

*** org-texinfo-tables-verbatim
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-texinfo-tables-verbatim
:END:

When non-nil, tables are exported verbatim.

- Default :: ~nil~
- Type :: ~boolean~

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-texinfo-tables-verbatim = false
#+end_src

*** org-texinfo-table-scientific-notation
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-texinfo-table-scientific-notation
:END:

Format string to display numbers in scientific notation.

The format should have "%s" twice, for mantissa and exponent
(i.e. "%s\\times10^{%s}").

When nil, no transformation is made.

- Default :: ~nil~
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (choice
   (string :tag "Format string")
   (const :tag "No formatting" nil))
  #+end_src

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-texinfo-table-scientific-notation = false
#+end_src

*** org-texinfo-table-default-markup
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-texinfo-table-default-markup
:END:

Default markup for first column in two-column tables.

This should an indicating command, e.g., "@code", "@kbd" or
"@samp".

It can be overridden locally using the ":indic" attribute.

- Default :: ~"@asis"~
- Type :: ~string~
- Since :: Org 9.1

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-texinfo-table-default-markup = "@asis"
#+end_src

*** org-texinfo-text-markup-alist
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-texinfo-text-markup-alist
:END:

Alist of Texinfo expressions to convert text markup.

The key must be a symbol among ~bold~, ~code~, ~italic~,
~strike-through~, ~underscore~ and ~verbatim~.  The value is
a formatting string to wrap fontified text with.

Value can also be set to the following symbols: ~verb~, ~samp~
and ~code~.  With the first one, Org uses "@verb" to create
a format string and selects a delimiter character that isn't in
the string.  For the other two, Org uses "@samp" or "@code"
to typeset and protects special characters.

When no association is found for a given markup, text is returned
as-is.

- Default ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  ((bold . "@strong{%s}")
   (code . code)
   (italic . "@emph{%s}")
   (verbatim . samp))
  #+end_src
- Type :: ~alist~
- Since :: Org 9.1

*** org-texinfo-format-drawer-function
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-texinfo-format-drawer-function
:END:

Function called to format a drawer in Texinfo code.

The function must accept two parameters:

#+begin_example
NAME      the drawer name, like "LOGBOOK"
CONTENTS  the contents of the drawer.
#+end_example

The function should return the string to be exported.

The default function simply returns the value of CONTENTS.

- Default ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (closure
      (org-texinfo-supports-math--cache t)
      (_name contents)
    contents)
  #+end_src
- Type :: ~function~
- Since :: Org 8.2

*** org-texinfo-format-inlinetask-function
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-texinfo-format-inlinetask-function
:END:

Function called to format an inlinetask in Texinfo code.

The function must accept six parameters:

#+begin_example
TODO      the todo keyword, as a string
TODO-TYPE the todo type, a symbol among `todo', `done' and nil.
PRIORITY  the inlinetask priority, as a string
NAME      the inlinetask name, as a string.
TAGS      the inlinetask tags, as a list of strings.
CONTENTS  the contents of the inlinetask, as a string.
#+end_example

The function should return the string to be exported.

- Default :: ~org-texinfo-format-inlinetask-default-function~
- Type :: ~function~

*** org-texinfo-with-latex
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-texinfo-with-latex
:END:

When non-nil, the Texinfo exporter attempts to process LaTeX math.

When set to t, the exporter will process LaTeX environments and
fragments as Texinfo "@displaymath" and "@math" commands
respectively.  Alternatively, when set to ~detect~, the exporter
does so only if the installed version of Texinfo supports the
necessary commands.

- Default :: ~detect~
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (choice
   (const :tag "Detect" detect)
   (const :tag "Yes" t)
   (const :tag "No" nil))
  #+end_src
- Since :: Org 9.6

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-texinfo-with-latex = "detect"
#+end_src

*** org-texinfo-compact-itemx
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-texinfo-compact-itemx
:END:

Non-nil means certain items in description list become ~@itemx~.

If this is non-nil and an item in a description list has no
body but is followed by another item, then the second item is
transcoded to ~@itemx~.  See info node `(org)Plain lists in
Texinfo export' for how to enable this for individual lists.

- Default :: ~nil~
- Type :: ~boolean~
- Since :: Org 9.6

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-texinfo-compact-itemx = false
#+end_src

*** org-texinfo-info-process
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-texinfo-info-process
:END:

Commands to process a Texinfo file to an INFO file.

This is a list of strings, each of them will be given to the
shell as a command.  %f in the command will be replaced by the
relative file name, %F by the absolute file name, %b by the file
base name (i.e. without directory and extension parts), %o by the
base directory of the file and %O by the absolute file name of
the output file.

Alternatively, this may be a Lisp function that does the processing,
This function should accept the file name as its single argument.

- Default :: ~("makeinfo --no-split %f")~
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (choice
   (repeat :tag "Shell command sequence"
           (string :tag "Shell command"))
   (function))
  #+end_src
- Since :: Org 9.1

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-texinfo-info-process = ["makeinfo --no-split %f"]
#+end_src

*** org-texinfo-logfiles-extensions
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-texinfo-logfiles-extensions
:END:

The list of file extensions to consider as Texinfo logfiles.
The logfiles will be remove if ~org-texinfo-remove-logfiles~ is
non-nil.

- Default :: ~("aux" "toc" "cp" "fn" "ky" "pg" "tp" "vr")~
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (repeat
   (string :tag "Extension"))
  #+end_src

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-texinfo-logfiles-extensions = ["aux", "toc", "cp", "fn", "ky", "pg", "tp", "vr"]
#+end_src

*** org-texinfo-remove-logfiles
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-texinfo-remove-logfiles
:END:

Non-nil means remove the logfiles produced by compiling a Texinfo file.
By default, logfiles are files with these extensions: .aux, .toc,
.cp, .fn, .ky, .pg and .tp.  To define the set of logfiles to remove,
set ~org-texinfo-logfiles-extensions~.

- Default :: ~t~
- Type :: ~boolean~

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-texinfo-remove-logfiles = true
#+end_src

** ox.el

*** org-export-with-archived-trees
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-with-archived-trees
:END:

Whether sub-trees with the ARCHIVE tag should be exported.

This can have three different values:

#+begin_example
nil         Do not export, pretend this tree is not present.
t           Do export the entire tree.
`headline'  Only export the headline, but skip the tree below it.
#+end_example

This option can also be set with the OPTIONS keyword,
e.g. "arch:nil".

- Default :: ~headline~
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (choice
   (const :tag "Not at all" nil)
   (const :tag "Headline only" headline)
   (const :tag "Entirely" t))
  #+end_src

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-export-with-archived-trees = "headline"
#+end_src

*** org-export-with-author
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-with-author
:END:

Non-nil means insert author name into the exported file.
This option can also be set with the OPTIONS keyword,
e.g. "author:nil".

- Default :: ~t~
- Type :: ~boolean~

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-export-with-author = true
#+end_src

This option can also be set with the OPTIONS keyword,
e.g. "c:t".

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-export-with-clocks = false
#+end_src

:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-with-creator
:END:

Non-nil means the postamble should contain a creator sentence.

The sentence can be set in ~org-export-creator-string~, which
see.

This option can also be set with the OPTIONS keyword, e.g.,
"creator:t".

- Default :: ~nil~
- Type :: ~boolean~
- Since :: Org 8.3

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-export-with-creator = false
#+end_src

*** org-export-with-date
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-with-date
:END:

Non-nil means insert date in the exported document.
This option can also be set with the OPTIONS keyword,
e.g. "date:nil".

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-export-with-date = true
#+end_src

:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-process-citations
:END:

Non-nil means process citations using citation processors.

#+end_src

*** org-export-date-timestamp-format
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-date-timestamp-format
:END:

Timestamp format string to use for DATE keyword.

The format string, when specified, only applies if date consists
in a single timestamp.  Otherwise its value will be ignored.

See ~format-time-string~ for details on how to build this
string.

- Default :: ~nil~
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (choice
   (string :tag "Timestamp format string")
   (const :tag "No format string" nil))
  #+end_src

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-export-date-timestamp-format = false
#+end_src

*** org-export-creator-string
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-creator-string
:END:

Information about the creator of the document.
This option can also be set on with the CREATOR keyword.

- Default :: computed when Org loads:
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (format "Emacs %s (Org mode %s)"
          emacs-version
          (if (fboundp 'org-version) (org-version) "unknown version"))
  #+end_src
- Type :: ~(string :tag "Creator string")~

*** org-export-with-drawers
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-with-drawers
:END:

Non-nil means export contents of standard drawers.

When t, all drawers are exported.  This may also be a list of
drawer names to export, as strings.  If that list starts with
~not~, only drawers with such names will be ignored.

This variable doesn't apply to properties drawers.  See
~org-export-with-properties~ instead.

This option can also be set with the OPTIONS keyword,
e.g. "d:nil".

- Default :: ~(not "LOGBOOK")~
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (choice
   (const :tag "All drawers" t)
   (const :tag "None" nil)
   (repeat :tag "Selected drawers"
           (string :tag "Drawer name"))
   (list :tag "Ignored drawers"
         (const :format "" not)
         (repeat :tag "Specify names of drawers to ignore during export" :inline t
                 (string :tag "Drawer name"))))
  #+end_src
- Since :: Org 8.0

*** org-export-with-email
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-with-email
:END:

Non-nil means insert author email into the exported file.
This option can also be set with the OPTIONS keyword,
e.g. "email:t".

- Default :: ~nil~
- Type :: ~boolean~

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-export-with-email = false
#+end_src

*** org-export-with-emphasize
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-with-emphasize
:END:

Non-nil means interpret ~*word*~, ~/word/~, ~_word_~ and ~+word+~.

If the export target supports emphasizing text, the word will be
typeset in bold, italic, with an underline or strike-through,
respectively.

This option can also be set with the OPTIONS keyword,
e.g. "*:nil".

- Default :: ~t~
- Type :: ~boolean~

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-export-with-emphasize = true
#+end_src

*** org-export-exclude-tags
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-exclude-tags
:END:

Tags that exclude a tree from export.

All trees carrying any of these tags will be excluded from
export.  This is without condition, so even subtrees inside that
carry one of the ~org-export-select-tags~ will be removed.

This option can also be set with the EXCLUDE_TAGS keyword.

- Default :: ~("noexport")~
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (repeat
   (string :tag "Tag"))
  #+end_src

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-export-exclude-tags = ["noexport"]
#+end_src

*** org-export-with-fixed-width
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-with-fixed-width
:END:

Non-nil means export lines starting with ":".
This option can also be set with the OPTIONS keyword,
e.g. "::nil".

- Default :: ~t~
- Type :: ~boolean~
- Since :: Org 8.0

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-export-with-fixed-width = true
#+end_src

*** org-export-with-footnotes
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-with-footnotes
:END:

Non-nil means Org footnotes should be exported.
This option can also be set with the OPTIONS keyword,
e.g. "f:nil".

- Default :: ~t~
- Type :: ~boolean~

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-export-with-footnotes = true
#+end_src

*** org-export-with-latex
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-with-latex
:END:

Non-nil means process LaTeX environments and fragments.

This option can also be set with the OPTIONS line,
e.g. "tex:verbatim".  Allowed values are:

#+begin_example
nil         Ignore math snippets.
`verbatim'  Keep everything in verbatim.
t           Allow export of math snippets.
#+end_example

- Default :: ~t~
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (choice
   (const :tag "Do not process math in any way" nil)
   (const :tag "Interpret math snippets" t)
   (const :tag "Leave math verbatim" verbatim))
  #+end_src
- Since :: Org 8.0

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-export-with-latex = true
#+end_src

*** org-export-headline-levels
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-headline-levels
:END:

This level and its ancestors will be exported as a headline.

Descendants of this level will usually produce itemized or
enumerated lists when exported, but backend behavior may differ.

This option can also be set with the OPTIONS keyword,
e.g. "H:2".

- Default :: ~3~
- Type :: ~integer~

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-export-headline-levels = 3
#+end_src

*** org-export-default-language
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-default-language
:END:

The default language for export and clocktable translations, as a string.
This may have an association in
~org-clock-clocktable-language-setup~,
~org-export-smart-quotes-alist~ and ~org-export-dictionary~.
This option can also be set with the LANGUAGE keyword.

- Default :: ~"en"~
- Type :: ~(string :tag "Language")~

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-export-default-language = "en"
#+end_src

*** org-export-preserve-breaks
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-preserve-breaks
:END:

Non-nil means preserve all line breaks when exporting.
This option can also be set with the OPTIONS keyword,
e.g. "~\n:t~".

- Default :: ~nil~
- Type :: ~boolean~

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-export-preserve-breaks = false
#+end_src

*** org-export-with-entities
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-with-entities
:END:

Non-nil means interpret entities when exporting.

For example, HTML export converts ~\alpha~ to &alpha; and ~\AA~ to
&Aring;.

For a list of supported names, see the constant ~org-entities~
and the user option ~org-entities-user~.

This option can also be set with the OPTIONS keyword,
e.g. "e:nil".

- Default :: ~t~
- Type :: ~boolean~

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-export-with-entities = true
#+end_src

*** org-export-with-inlinetasks
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-with-inlinetasks
:END:

Non-nil means inlinetasks should be exported.
This option can also be set with the OPTIONS keyword,
e.g. "inline:nil".

- Default :: ~t~
- Type :: ~boolean~
- Since :: Org 8.0

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-export-with-inlinetasks = true
#+end_src

*** org-export-with-planning
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-with-planning
:END:

Non-nil means include planning info in export.

Planning info is the line containing either SCHEDULED:,
DEADLINE:, CLOSED: timestamps, or a combination of them.

This option can also be set with the OPTIONS keyword,
e.g. "p:t".

- Default :: ~nil~
- Type :: ~boolean~
- Since :: Org 8.0

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-export-with-planning = false
#+end_src

:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-with-priority
:END:

Non-nil means include priority cookies in export.
This option can also be set with the OPTIONS keyword,
e.g. "pri:t".

- Default :: ~nil~
- Type :: ~boolean~

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-export-with-priority = false
#+end_src

*** org-export-with-properties
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-with-properties
:END:

Non-nil means export contents of properties drawers.

When t, all properties are exported.  This may also be a list of
properties to export, as strings.

This option can also be set with the OPTIONS keyword,
e.g. "prop:t".

- Default :: ~nil~
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (choice
   (const :tag "All properties" t)
   (const :tag "None" nil)
   (repeat :tag "Selected properties"
           (string :tag "Property name")))
  #+end_src
- Since :: Org 8.3

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-export-with-properties = []
#+end_src

*** org-export-with-section-numbers
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-with-section-numbers
:END:

Non-nil means add section numbers to headlines when exporting.

When set to an integer n, numbering will only happen for
headlines whose relative level is higher or equal to n.

This option can also be set with the OPTIONS keyword,
e.g. "num:t".

- Default :: ~t~
- Type :: ~boolean~

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-export-with-section-numbers = true
#+end_src

*** org-export-select-tags
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-select-tags
:END:

Tags that select a tree for export.

If any such tag is found in a buffer, all trees that do not carry
one of these tags will be ignored during export.  Inside trees
that are selected like this, you can still deselect a subtree by
tagging it with one of the ~org-export-exclude-tags~.

This option can also be set with the SELECT_TAGS keyword.

- Default :: ~("export")~
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (repeat
   (string :tag "Tag"))
  #+end_src

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-export-select-tags = ["export"]
#+end_src

*** org-export-with-smart-quotes
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-with-smart-quotes
:END:

Non-nil means activate smart quotes during export.
This option can also be set with the OPTIONS keyword,
e.g., "':t".

When setting this to non-nil, you need to take care of
using the correct Babel package when exporting to LaTeX.
E.g., you can load Babel for french like this:

#+begin_example
,#+LATEX_HEADER: \usepackage[french]{babel}
#+end_example

- Default :: ~nil~
- Type :: ~boolean~
- Since :: Org 8.0

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-export-with-smart-quotes = false
#+end_src

*** org-export-with-special-strings
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-with-special-strings
:END:

Non-nil means interpret "~\-~", "--" and "---" for export.

When this option is turned on, these strings will be exported as:

#+begin_example
 Org     HTML     LaTeX    UTF-8
-----+----------+--------+-------
 \-    &shy;      \-
 --    &ndash;    --         –
 ---   &mdash;    ---        —
 ...   &hellip;   \ldots     …
#+end_example

This option can also be set with the OPTIONS keyword,
e.g. "-:nil".

- Default :: ~t~
- Type :: ~boolean~

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-export-with-special-strings = true
#+end_src

*** org-export-with-statistics-cookies
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-with-statistics-cookies
:END:

Non-nil means include statistics cookies in export.
This option can also be set with the OPTIONS keyword,
e.g. "stat:nil"

- Default :: ~t~
- Type :: ~boolean~
- Since :: Org 8.0

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-export-with-statistics-cookies = true
#+end_src

*** org-export-with-sub-superscripts
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-with-sub-superscripts
:END:

Non-nil means interpret "_" and "^" for export.

If you want to control how Org displays those characters, see
~org-use-sub-superscripts~.

When this option is turned on, you can use TeX-like syntax for
sub- and superscripts and see them exported correctly.

You can also set the option with #+OPTIONS: ^:t

See ~org-use-sub-superscripts~ docstring for more details.

- Default :: ~t~
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (choice
   (const :tag "Interpret them" t)
   (const :tag "Curly brackets only" {})
   (const :tag "Do not interpret them" nil))
  #+end_src
- Since :: Org 8.0

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-export-with-sub-superscripts = true
#+end_src

*** org-export-with-toc
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-with-toc
:END:

Non-nil means create a table of contents in exported files.

The table of contents contains headlines with levels up to
~org-export-headline-levels~.

When this variable is set to an integer N, include levels up to
N in the table of contents.  Although it may then be different
from ~org-export-headline-levels~, it is cannot be larger than
the number of headline levels.

When nil, no table of contents is created.

This option can also be set with the OPTIONS keyword,
e.g. "toc:nil" or "toc:3".

- Default :: ~t~
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (choice
   (const :tag "No Table of Contents" nil)
   (const :tag "Full Table of Contents" t)
   (integer :tag "TOC to level"))
  #+end_src

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-export-with-toc = true
#+end_src

*** org-export-with-tables
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-with-tables
:END:

Non-nil means export tables.
This option can also be set with the OPTIONS keyword,
e.g. "|:nil".

- Default :: ~t~
- Type :: ~boolean~
- Since :: Org 8.0

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-export-with-tables = true
#+end_src

*** org-export-with-tags
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-with-tags
:END:

If nil, do not export tags, just remove them from headlines.

If this is the symbol ~not-in-toc~, tags will be removed from
table of contents entries, but still be shown in the headlines of
the document.

This option can also be set with the OPTIONS keyword,
e.g. "tags:nil".

- Default :: ~t~
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (choice
   (const :tag "Off" nil)
   (const :tag "Not in TOC" not-in-toc)
   (const :tag "On" t))
  #+end_src

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-export-with-tags = true
#+end_src

*** org-export-with-tasks
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-with-tasks
:END:

Non-nil means include TODO items for export.

This may have the following values:

#+begin_example
t                    include tasks independent of state.
`todo'               include only tasks that are not yet done.
`done'               include only tasks that are already done.
nil                  ignore all tasks.
#+end_example

list of keywords     include tasks with these keywords.

This option can also be set with the OPTIONS keyword,
e.g. "tasks:nil".

- Default :: ~t~
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (choice
   (const :tag "All tasks" t)
   (const :tag "No tasks" nil)
   (const :tag "Not-done tasks" todo)
   (const :tag "Only done tasks" done)
   (repeat :tag "Specific TODO keywords"
           (string :tag "Keyword")))
  #+end_src

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-export-with-tasks = true
#+end_src

*** org-export-with-title
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-with-title
:END:

Non-nil means print title into the exported file.
This option can also be set with the OPTIONS keyword,
e.g. "title:nil".

- Default :: ~t~
- Type :: ~boolean~
- Since :: Org 8.3

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-export-with-title = true
#+end_src

*** org-export-timestamp-file
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-timestamp-file
:END:

Non-nil means insert a time stamp into the exported file.
The time stamp shows when the file was created.  This option can
also be set with the OPTIONS keyword, e.g. "timestamp:nil".

- Default :: ~t~
- Type :: ~boolean~

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-export-timestamp-file = true
#+end_src

*** org-export-with-timestamps
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-with-timestamps
:END:

Non-nil means allow timestamps in export.

It can be set to any of the following values:

#+begin_example
t                        export all timestamps.
`active'                 export active timestamps, including diary timestamps.
`active-exclude-diary'   export active timestamps, excluding diary timestamps.
`inactive'               export inactive timestamps only.
nil                      do not export timestamps
#+end_example

This only applies to timestamps isolated in a paragraph
containing only timestamps.  Other timestamps are always
exported.

This option can also be set with the OPTIONS keyword, e.g.
"<:nil".

- Default :: ~t~
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (choice
   (const :tag "All timestamps" t)
   (const :tag "Active timestamps, including diary timestamps" active)
   (const :tag "Active timestamps, excluding diary timestamps" active-exclude-diary)
   (const :tag "Only inactive timestamps" inactive)
   (const :tag "No timestamp" nil))
  #+end_src
- Since :: Org 9.8

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-export-with-timestamps = true
#+end_src

*** org-export-with-todo-keywords
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-with-todo-keywords
:END:

Non-nil means include TODO keywords in export.
When nil, remove all these keywords from the export.  This option
can also be set with the OPTIONS keyword, e.g.  "todo:nil".

- Default :: ~t~
- Type :: ~boolean~

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-export-with-todo-keywords = true
#+end_src

*** org-export-allow-bind-keywords
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-allow-bind-keywords
:END:

Non-nil means BIND keywords can define local variable values.
This is a potential security risk, which is why the default value
is nil.  You can also allow them through local buffer variables.

- Default :: ~nil~
- Type :: ~boolean~
- Since :: Org 8.0

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-export-allow-bind-keywords = false
#+end_src

*** org-export-with-broken-links
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-with-broken-links
:END:

Non-nil means do not raise an error on broken links.

When this variable is non-nil, broken links are ignored, without
stopping the export process.  If it is set to ~mark~, broken
links are marked as such in the output, with a string like

#+begin_example
[BROKEN LINK: path]
#+end_example

where PATH is the un-resolvable reference.

This option can also be set with the OPTIONS keyword, e.g.,
"broken-links:mark".

- Default :: ~nil~
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (choice
   (const :tag "Ignore broken links" t)
   (const :tag "Mark broken links in output" mark)
   (const :tag "Raise an error" nil))
  #+end_src
- Since :: Org 9.0

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-export-with-broken-links = false
#+end_src

:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-expand-links
:END:

When non-nil, expand environment variables in file paths.

- Default :: ~t~
- Type :: ~boolean~
- Since :: Org 9.7

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-export-expand-links = true
#+end_src

*** org-export-snippet-translation-alist
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-snippet-translation-alist
:END:

Alist between export snippets backends and exporter backends.

This variable allows providing shortcuts for export snippets.

For example, with:

#+begin_example
(setq org-export-snippet-translation-alist
      '(("h" . "html")))
#+end_example

the HTML backend will recognize the contents of "~@@h:<b>@@~" as
HTML code while every other backend will ignore it.

- Default :: ~nil~
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (repeat
   (cons
    (string :tag "Shortcut")
    (string :tag "Backend")))
  #+end_src
- Since :: Org 8.0

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-export-snippet-translation-alist = []
#+end_src

*** org-export-global-macros
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-global-macros
:END:

Alist between macro names and expansion templates.

This variable defines macro expansion templates available
globally.  Associations follow the pattern

#+begin_example
(NAME . TEMPLATE)
#+end_example

where NAME is a string beginning with a letter and consisting of
alphanumeric characters only.

TEMPLATE is the string to which the macro is going to be
expanded.  Inside, "$1", "$2"... are place-holders for
macro's arguments.  Moreover, if the template starts with
"(eval", it will be parsed as an Elisp expression and evaluated
accordingly.

- Default :: ~nil~
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (repeat
   (cons
    (string :tag "Name")
    (string :tag "Template")))
  #+end_src
- Since :: Org 9.1

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-export-global-macros = []
#+end_src

*** org-export-show-temporary-export-buffer
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-show-temporary-export-buffer
:END:

Non-nil means show buffer after exporting to temp buffer.
When Org exports to a file, the buffer visiting that file is never
shown, but remains buried.  However, when exporting to
a temporary buffer, that buffer is popped up in a second window.
When this variable is nil, the buffer remains buried also in
these cases.

- Default :: ~t~
- Type :: ~boolean~

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-export-show-temporary-export-buffer = true
#+end_src

:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-body-only
:END:

The initial "Body only" setting when exporting with ~org-export-dispatch~.
Non-nil means only export body code, without the surrounding
template.

- Type :: ~boolean~
- Since :: Org 9.7

Non-nil means export and publishing commands will run in background.

*** org-export-async-init-file
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-async-init-file
:END:

File used to initialize external export process.

Value must be either nil or an absolute file name.  When nil, the
external process is launched like a regular Emacs session,
loading user's initialization file and any site specific
configuration.  If a file is provided, it, and only it, is loaded
at start-up.

Therefore, using a specific configuration makes the process to
load faster and the export more portable.

- Default :: ~nil~
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (choice
   (const :tag "Regular startup" nil)
   (file :tag "Specific start-up file" :must-match t))
  #+end_src
- Since :: Org 8.0

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-export-async-init-file = false
#+end_src

*** org-export-dispatch-use-expert-ui
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-dispatch-use-expert-ui
:END:

Non-nil means using a non-intrusive ~org-export-dispatch~.
In that case, no help buffer is displayed.  Though, an indicator
for current export scope is added to the prompt ("b" when
output is restricted to body only, "s" when it is restricted to
the current subtree, "v" when only visible elements are
considered for export, "f" when publishing functions should be
passed the FORCE argument and "a" when the export should be
asynchronous).  Also, [?] allows switching back to standard
mode.

- Default :: ~nil~
- Type :: ~boolean~
- Since :: Org 8.0

#+begin_src toml
[emacs_options]
org-export-dispatch-use-expert-ui = false
#+end_src

*** org-export-smart-quotes-alist
:PROPERTIES:
:CUSTOM_ID: org-export-smart-quotes-alist
:END:

Smart quotes translations.

Alist whose CAR is a language string and CDR is an alist with
quote type as key and a plist associating various encodings to
their translation as value.

A quote type can be any symbol among ~primary-opening~,
~primary-closing~, ~secondary-opening~, ~secondary-closing~ and
~apostrophe~.

Valid encodings include ~:utf-8~, ~:html~, ~:latex~ and
~:texinfo~.

If no translation is found, the quote character is left as-is.

- Default ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (("ar"
    (primary-opening :utf-8 "«" :html "&laquo;" :latex "\\guillemotleft{}" :texinfo "@guillemetleft{}")
    (primary-closing :utf-8 "»" :html "&raquo;" :latex "\\guillemotright{}" :texinfo "@guillemetright{}")
    (secondary-opening :utf-8 "‹" :html "&lsaquo;" :latex "\\guilsinglleft{}" :texinfo "@guilsinglleft{}")
    (secondary-closing :utf-8 "›" :html "&rsaquo;" :latex "\\guilsinglright{}" :texinfo "@guilsinglright{}")
    (apostrophe :utf-8 "’" :html "&rsquo;"))
   ("da"
    (primary-opening :utf-8 "»" :html "&raquo;" :latex ">>" :texinfo "@guillemetright{}")
    (primary-closing :utf-8 "«" :html "&laquo;" :latex "<<" :texinfo "@guillemetleft{}")
    (secondary-opening :utf-8 "›" :html "&rsaquo;" :latex "\\frq{}" :texinfo "@guilsinglright{}")
    (secondary-closing :utf-8 "‹" :html "&lsaquo;" :latex "\\flq{}" :texinfo "@guilsingleft{}")
    (apostrophe :utf-8 "’" :html "&rsquo;"))
   ("de"
    (primary-opening :utf-8 "„" :html "&bdquo;" :latex "\"`" :texinfo "@quotedblbase{}")
    (primary-closing :utf-8 "“" :html "&ldquo;" :latex "\"'" :texinfo "@quotedblleft{}")
    (secondary-opening :utf-8 "‚" :html "&sbquo;" :latex "\\glq{}" :texinfo "@quotesinglbase{}")
    (secondary-closing :utf-8 "‘" :html "&lsquo;" :latex "\\grq{}" :texinfo "@quoteleft{}")
    (apostrophe :utf-8 "’" :html "&rsquo;"))
   ("el"
  …
  #+end_src
- Type ::
  #+begin_src emacs-lisp
  (alist :key-type
         (string :tag "Language name")
         :value-type
         (alist :key-type
                (choice
                 (const :tag "Primary opening" primary-opening)
                 (const :tag "Primary closing" primary-closing)
                 (const :tag "Secondary opening" secondary-opening)
                 (const :tag "Secondary closing" secondary-closing)
                 (const :tag "Apostrophe" apostrophe))
                :value-type
  …
  #+end_src
- Since :: Org 9.7

# END generated
